We let $\mathcal{S}$ denote the set of star-cut step graphons, $\mathcal{C}$ the set of cycle-cut, $\mathcal{R}$ the set of reflective-cut, $\mathcal{Q}$ the set of quad-cut. We say $W$ is value-extremal if $W\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{Q}$ .
Remark 8.6.
It turns out the above are precisely the extremal graphons for our problem (those exactly attaining a value of $1/4$ ) but since we do not require this fact nor rely on it for our stability proof, we do not prove this. The reader should also note that in the latter three families, the conditions for $\mu$ become increasingly more restrictive while those for $\varphi$ become increasingly more lax. In fact, the latter two actually arise from the cycle-cut family via the symmetry of $\mu$ ; namely if two or even three cycle-cut graphons can be formed on the same part sizes, then their average is also a triangle-thin step graphon with value $1/4$ .
8.1 Value Stability for Four Symmetrized Parts
We are now prepared to state our stability version of Lemma 5.2 as follows.
Lemma 8.7 (Value Stability for 4 Symmetrized Parts).
There exists $\varepsilon_{0}>0$ such that for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$ the following holds: If $W=(K_{4},\mu,\varphi)$ is a triangle-thin step graphon with value at least $\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ , then $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to a value-extremal step graphon.
Proof.
We choose $\varepsilon_{0}>0$ small enough as needed so that various inequalities throughout the proof regarding $\varepsilon$ hold true. Let $x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}$ be an enumeration of $V(K_{4})$ such that $\mu(x_{1})\geq\mu(x_{2})\geq\mu(x_{3})\geq\mu(x_{4})$ ; for brevity, we let $m_{i}:=\mu(x_{i})$ for $i\in[4]$ and $a_{ij}:=\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})$ for distinct $i<j\in[4]$ . (Recall that this can fundamentally be viewed as graph Lagrangian problem wherein we are characterizing the extremal of the quadratic form $m^{T}\cdot A\cdot m$ where $m$ is a vector with entries $m_{i}$ and $A$ is the matrix with entries $a_{ij}$ and $a_{ii}=0$ for $i\in[4]$ ).
Define $b_{3}:=\varepsilon^{0.02}$ , $b_{4}:=\varepsilon^{0.06}$ , $d_{2}:=\varepsilon^{0.08}$ , $d_{4}:=\varepsilon^{0.02}$ . We will consider different cases based on whether $m_{3}$ is larger or smaller than $b_{3}$ , whether $m_{4}$ is larger or smaller than $b_{4}$ , whether $|m_{1}-m_{2}|$ is larger or smaller than $d_{2}$ , and whether $|m_{1}-m_{4}|$ is larger or smaller than $d_{4}$ . For each case, we will present a value-extremal step graphon $W^{*}=(K_{4},\mu^{*},\varphi^{*})$ that is close to $W$ ; again for brevity, we write $m_{i}^{*}:=\mu^{*}(x_{i})$ for $i\in[4]$ and $a_{ij}^{*}:=\varphi^{*}(x_{i}x_{j})$ for distinct $i<j\in[4]$ (note we use the identity isomorphism in each case). For most cases, we will also first present a graphon $W^{\prime}=(K_{4},\mu^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})$ with $\varphi^{\prime}:=\varphi$ and with $|\mu^{\prime}(x_{i})-\mu(x_{i})|\leq\sigma$ for some small $\sigma>0$ (indeed, $\mu^{\prime}(x_{i})$ will equal $\mu^{*}(x_{i})$ ). Note then that $W$ is $\sigma$ -close to $W$ . An important observation then for all these cases is that $|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|-2\sigma\cdot\sum_{i<j\in[4]}a_{ij}\geq|W|-12\sigma$ . We then will show that $W^{\prime}$ is close to $W^{*}$ from whence it follows that $W$ is also close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
Claim 8.7.1.
$|m_{1}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ or $|m_{1}+m_{4}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ .
Proof. Suppose not. As $|m_{1}-\frac{1}{2}|>\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ , we find that $m_{1}(m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4})<\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ . Similarly as $|m_{1}+m_{4}-\frac{1}{2}|>\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ , we find that $(m_{1}+m_{4})(m_{2}+m_{3})<\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ . But then it follows from Lemma 5.2, that the value of $W$ is strictly less than $\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ , a contradiction.
$\Diamond$
Note that by Claim 8.7.1, we find that $m_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and hence $m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . Since $m_{2}\geq m_{3}\geq m_{4}$ , we find that $m_{2}\geq\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{3}>0$ (since $\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}$ ).
Claim 8.7.2.
$m_{3}\geq b_{3}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $m_{3}<b_{3}$ . We let $W^{*}:=(K_{4},\mu^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})$ be defined as $m_{1}^{*}=m_{2}^{*}:=\frac{1}{2}$ , $m_{3}^{*}=m_{4}^{*}:=0$ ; $a_{12}^{*}=1$ , $a_{ij}^{*}=a_{ij}$ for distinct $i<j\in[4]$ where $\{i,j\}\neq\{1,2\}$ . Note that $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal step graphon (specifically it is star-cut). Note that $|m_{i}-m_{i}^{*}|\leq b_{3}$ for $i\in\{3,4\}$ . By Claim 8.7.1, we have that $m_{1}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and $m_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ ; hence $|m_{1}-m_{1}^{*}|=|m_{1}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{3}\leq 2b_{3}$ . Similarly $m_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and $m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and hence $|m_{2}-m_{2}^{*}|=|m_{2}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+2b_{3}\leq 3b_{3}$ .
By assumption $|W|\geq\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ . Yet $|W|\leq a_{12}m_{1}m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}\leq a_{12}\cdot\frac{1}{4}+2b_{3}$ where we used that $m_{4}\leq m_{3}\leq b_{3}$ and $m_{1}m_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}$ . By rearranging, we find that $a_{12}\geq 1-4\varepsilon-8b_{3}$ . Thus $|a_{12}-a_{12}^{*}|\leq 4\varepsilon+8b_{3}\leq 12b_{3}$ . Combining all our observations, we find that $W$ is $12b_{3}$ -close to $W^{*}$ ; since $12b_{3}=12\cdot\varepsilon^{0.02}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, we find that $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
Claim 8.7.3.
$m_{4}\geq b_{4}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $m_{4}<b_{4}$ . We let $W^{*}=(K_{4},\mu^{*},\varphi^{*})$ be defined as $m_{1}^{*}:=\frac{1}{2}$ , $m_{2}^{*}:=\frac{m_{2}}{2(m_{2}+m_{3})}$ , $m_{3}^{*}:=\frac{m_{3}}{2(m_{2}+m_{3})}$ , $m_{4}^{*}:=0$ ; $a_{12}^{*}:=a_{13}^{*}:=1$ , $a_{23}^{*}:=0$ $a_{i4}^{*}=a_{i4}$ for $i\in[3]$ . (Recall this is well-defined as $m_{2}\geq m_{3}\geq b_{3}>0$ .) Note that $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal step graphon (specifically it is star-cut). Note that $|m_{4}-m_{4}^{*}|\leq b_{4}$ . By Claim 8.7.1, we have that $m_{1}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and $m_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ ; hence $|m_{1}-m_{1}^{*}|=|x_{1}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{4}\leq 2b_{4}$ . Similarly $m_{2}+m_{3}=1-(m_{1}+m_{4})\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{4}$ and $m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and hence $|m_{2}+m_{3}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{4}\leq 2b_{4}$ . Thus $|m_{2}-m_{2}^{*}|=|m_{2}+m_{3}-\frac{1}{2}|\cdot\frac{m_{2}}{m_{2}+m_{3}}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{4}\leq 2b_{4}$ and similarly $|m_{3}-m_{3}^{*}|=|m_{2}+m_{3}-\frac{1}{2}|\cdot\frac{m_{3}}{m_{2}+m_{3}}\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+b_{4}\leq 2b_{4}$ .
By assumption $|W|\geq\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon$ . Yet $|W|\leq a_{12}m_{1}m_{2}+a_{13}m_{2}m_{3}+a_{23}m_{2}m_{3}+m_{4}$ . Substituting that $m_{4}\leq b_{4}$ by assumption and $a_{23}\leq 2-a_{12}-a_{13}$ by triangle-thinness of $W$ , we find that
$\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon-b_{4}$
$\displaystyle\leq a_{12}m_{1}m_{2}+a_{13}m_{1}m_{3}+(2-a_{12}-a_{13})m_{2}m_{3}$
$\displaystyle=m_{1}m_{2}+m_{1}m_{3}-(1-a_{12})m_{2}(m_{1}-m_{3})-(1-a_{13})m_{3}(m_{1}-m_{2})$
$\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}-(1-a_{12})\cdot b_{3}\cdot\frac{b_{3}}{2}-(1-a_{13})\cdot b_{3}\cdot\frac{b_{3}}{2},$
where we used that $m_{2}\geq m_{3}\geq b_{3}$ , that $m_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}-b_{4}$ and $m_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}-b_{3}$ and hence $m_{1}-m_{3}\geq m_{1}-m_{2}\geq b_{3}-b_{4}-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geq b_{3}-3b_{4}\geq\frac{b_{3}}{2}$ as $b_{3}=\varepsilon^{0.02}$ , $b_{4}=\varepsilon^{0.06}$ , and $\varepsilon$ is small enough. Rearranging this gives
$(2-a_{12}-a_{13})\leq 2\cdot\frac{b_{4}+\varepsilon}{b_{3}^{2}}\leq 4\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{0.06}}{(\varepsilon^{0.02})^{2}}\leq 4\varepsilon^{0.02}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01},$
where the last inequality follows since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
Thus we find that $a_{12}\geq 1-\varepsilon^{0.01}$ , $a_{13}\geq 1-\varepsilon^{0.01}$ and $a_{23}\leq 2-a_{12}-a_{23}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ . Thus $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
Claim 8.7.4.
$m_{1}<\frac{1}{2}-\frac{b_{4}}{2}$
Proof. Suppose not, that is $m_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{b_{4}}{2}$ . Since $m_{4}\geq b_{4}$ by Claim 8.7.3, we have that $m_{1}+m_{4}\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{b_{4}}{2}>\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ since $b_{4}>2\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough. But then by Claim 8.7.1, we find that $m_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . Let $W^{\prime}$ be defined as $m_{1}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{2}$ and for $j\in\{2,3,4\}$ , $m_{j}^{\prime}:=\frac{m_{j}}{2(m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4})}$ , and $a_{ij}^{\prime}=a_{ij}$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ ; we will use later the fact that here $m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime}=m_{4}^{\prime}$ . Hence $|m_{1}-m_{1}^{\prime}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . This also implies that $|m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . But then $\frac{1}{2(m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4})}\geq\frac{1}{1+2\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\geq 1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and similarly $\frac{1}{2(m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4})}\leq\frac{1}{1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\leq 1+3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ (since $\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geq 6\varepsilon$ as $\varepsilon\leq 1/36$ ). Thus we find that $|m_{i}-m_{i}^{\prime}|\leq 3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ for all $i\in\{2,3,4\}$ . Thus $W$ is $3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ . Moreover, we find that $|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|-36\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ .
Let $W^{*}$ be defined as $m_{i}^{*}:=m_{i}^{\prime}$ for all $i\in[4]$ , $a_{1i}^{*}:=1$ for $i\in\{2,3,4\}$ and $a_{23}^{*}=a_{24}^{*}=a_{34}^{*}:=0$ . Note that $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal step graphon (specifically it is star-cut). Hence $|W^{*}|=\frac{1}{4}$ . Thus $|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+36\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . On the other hand, we calculate that
$\displaystyle|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i\in\{2,3,4\}}(1-a_{1i})m_{1}^{\prime}m_{i}^{\prime}\bigg)-a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-a_{24}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-a_{34}m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}$
$\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\sum_{i\in\{2,3,4\}}(1-a_{1i})m_{1}^{\prime}m_{i}^{\prime}\bigg)-(2-a_{12}-a_{13})m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-(2-a_{12}-a_{14})m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-(2-a_{13}-a_{14})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}$
$\displaystyle=(1-a_{12})m_{2}^{\prime}(m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime})+(1-a_{13})m_{3}^{\prime}(m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime})+(1-a_{14})m_{4}^{\prime}(m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime})$
$\displaystyle\geq(3-a_{12}-a_{13}-a_{14})b_{4}\cdot b_{4}\cdot(1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}),$
where we used triangle–thinness for upper bounds on $a_{23},a_{24},a_{34}$ , and that $m_{2}^{\prime}\geq m_{3}^{\prime}\geq m_{4}^{\prime}\geq b_{4}$ and that $m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime}\geq m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime}\geq m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime}=m_{4}^{\prime}\geq m_{4}(1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon})\geq b_{4}(1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon})$ . Thus we find that $a_{12}+a_{13}+a_{14}\geq 3-2\cdot\frac{\varepsilon+36\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{b_{4}^{2}}\geq 3-74\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{(\varepsilon^{0.06})^{2}}\geq 3-\varepsilon^{0.30}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough. Since $a_{ij}\leq 1$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ , we find that $a_{1i}\geq 1-\varepsilon^{0.30}$ . Similarly, it follows that $a_{23},a_{24},a_{34}\leq\varepsilon^{0.30}$ . Thus $W^{\prime}$ is $\varepsilon^{0.30}$ -close to $W^{*}$ . Hence $W$ is $(3\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{0.30})$ -close to $W^{*}$ ; since $3\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{0.30}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough we find that $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
By Claim 8.7.1 since $m_{1}<\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ , we find that $|m_{1}+m_{4}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ .
Claim 8.7.5.
$m_{1}-m_{2}\leq d_{2}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $m_{1}-m_{2}>d_{2}$ . Let $W^{\prime}$ be defined as $m_{1}^{\prime}:=\frac{m_{1}}{2(m_{1}+m_{4})}$ , $m_{4}^{\prime}:=\frac{m_{4}}{2(m_{1}+m_{4})}$ , $m_{2}^{\prime}:=\frac{m_{2}}{2(m_{2}+m_{3})}$ , $m_{3}^{\prime}:=\frac{m_{3}}{2(m_{2}+m_{3})}$ and $a_{ij}^{\prime}=a_{ij}$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ . Since $|m_{1}+m_{4}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ , we find that $\frac{1}{2(m_{1}+m_{4})}\geq\frac{1}{1+2\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\geq 1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and similarly $\frac{1}{2(m_{1}+m_{4})}\leq\frac{1}{1-2\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\leq 1+3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ (since $\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geq 6\varepsilon$ as $\varepsilon\leq 1/36$ ). It follows then $|m_{4}-m_{4}^{\prime}|\leq|m_{1}-m_{1}^{\prime}|\leq 3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . Similarly $|m_{2}+m_{3}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and via the same argument that $|m_{3}-m_{3}^{\prime}|\leq|m_{2}-m_{2}^{\prime}|\leq 3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . Thus $W$ is $3\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ and we also find that $|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|-36\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ .
Let $W^{*}$ be defined as $m_{i}^{*}:=m_{i}^{\prime}$ for $i\in[4]$ and $a_{12}^{*}=a_{13}^{*}=a_{24}^{*}=a_{34}^{*}:=1$ and $a_{14}^{*}=a_{23}^{*}=0$ . Note that $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal step graphon (specifically it is cycle-cut). Hence $|W^{*}|=\frac{1}{4}$ . Thus $|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon+36\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ . On the other hand, we calculate that
$\displaystyle|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i\in\{1,4\}}\sum_{j\in\{2,3\}}(1-a_{ij})m_{i}^{\prime}m_{j}^{\prime}\bigg)-a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-a_{14}m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}.$
Let $\delta:=\varepsilon^{0.32}$ and $\delta^{\prime}:=\varepsilon^{0.15}$ . Now we case according to how $a_{23},a_{14}$ compare to $\delta$ and $\delta^{\prime}$ . First suppose that $a_{23},a_{14}<\delta^{\prime}$ . Then $a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}+a_{14}m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}<2\delta^{\prime}$ . It follows that for all $i\in\{1,4\}$ and $j\in\{2,3\}$ with $1-a_{ij}\leq\frac{36\sqrt{\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime}}{b_{4}^{2}}$ as otherwise we obtain a contradiction. But then $W^{\prime}$ is $\frac{36\sqrt{\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime}}{b_{4}^{2}}$ -close to $W^{*}$ and hence $W$ is $\frac{72\sqrt{\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime}}{b_{4}^{2}}$ -close to $W^{*}$ ; since $\frac{72\sqrt{\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime}}{b_{4}^{2}}\leq 74\cdot\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{b_{4}^{2}}=74\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{0.21}}{(\varepsilon^{0.099})^{2}}=74\cdot\varepsilon^{0.012}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, we find that $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired. So we assume at least one of $a_{23}\geq\delta^{\prime}$ or $a_{14}\geq\delta^{\prime}$ holds.
Next suppose that $a_{23}\geq\delta^{\prime}$ and $a_{14}\leq\delta$ . But then we calculate using that $m_{i}^{\prime}\geq m_{j}^{\prime}$ for $i<j\in[4]$ and using both that $a_{23}\leq 2-a_{12}-a_{13}$ as well that $a_{23}\leq 2-a_{24}-a_{34}$ by triangle-thinness that
$\displaystyle|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle\geq(1-a_{12})m_{1}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}+(1-a_{13})m_{1}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}+(1-a_{24})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}+(1-a_{34})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-a_{14}m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}$
$\displaystyle\geq(2-a_{12}-a_{13})m_{1}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}+(2-a_{24}-a_{34})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-\delta$
$\displaystyle\geq a_{23}(m_{1}^{\prime}+m_{4}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime})m_{3}^{\prime}-\delta$
$\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\prime}\cdot d_{2}\cdot\frac{b_{3}}{2}-\delta=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{0.15}\cdot\varepsilon^{0.08}\cdot\varepsilon^{0.02}-\varepsilon^{0.32}>36\sqrt{\varepsilon},$
a contradiction where we used that $m_{3}^{\prime}\geq\frac{m_{3}}{2}\geq\frac{b_{3}}{2}$ and the last inequality follows since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
Similarly we suppose that $a_{14}\geq\delta^{\prime}$ and $a_{23}\leq\delta$ . But then we calculate using that $m_{i}^{\prime}\geq m_{j}^{\prime}$ for $i<j\in[4]$ and using both that $a_{14}\leq 2-a_{12}-a_{24}$ and $a_{14}\leq 2-a_{13}-a_{34}$ by triangle-thinness that
$\displaystyle|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle\geq(1-a_{12})m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}+(1-a_{13})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}+(1-a_{24})m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}+(1-a_{34})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-a_{23}m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}-a_{14}m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}$
$\displaystyle\geq(2-a_{12}-a_{24})m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}+(2-a_{13}-a_{34})m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-a_{14}m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-\delta$
$\displaystyle\geq a_{14}(m_{2}^{\prime}+m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{1}^{\prime})m_{4}^{\prime}-\delta$
$\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\prime}\cdot\frac{b_{4}}{2}\cdot\frac{b_{4}}{2}-\delta=\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{0.15}\cdot(\varepsilon^{0.06})^{2}-\varepsilon^{0.32}>36\sqrt{\varepsilon},$
a contradiction where we used that $m_{4}^{\prime}\geq\frac{b_{4}}{2}$ and hence $m_{2}^{\prime}+m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{1}^{\prime}=m_{4}^{\prime}\geq\frac{b_{4}}{2}$ and the last inequality follows since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
Finally we assume that $a_{14},a_{23}\geq\delta$ . Let $M_{1}:=\{14,23\},M_{2}:=\{24,13\},M_{3}:=\{34,12\}$ . Let $S:=\{ij\in M_{2}\cup M_{3}:1-a_{ij}\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\}$ . Since $2-a_{12}-a_{13}\geq a_{23}$ by triangle-thinness and $a_{23}\geq\delta$ , it follows that $|S\cap\{12,13\}|\geq 1$ . Similarly since $2-a_{24}-a_{34}\geq a_{23}\geq\delta$ , it follows that $|S\cap\{24,34\}|\geq 1$ . As $2-a_{12}-a_{14}\geq a_{14}\geq\delta$ , it follows that $|S\cap\{12,24\}|\geq 1$ . As $2-a_{13}-a_{34}\geq a_{14}\geq\delta$ , it follows that $|S\cap\{13,34\}|\geq 1$ . Combining these observations, we find that $M_{2}\subseteq S$ or $M_{3}\subseteq S$ .
Choose $i\in\{2,3\}$ with $M_{i}\subseteq S$ . Let $W^{+}$ be a step graphon defined as $m^{+}_{i}=m^{\prime}_{i}$ for $i\in[4]$ , $a_{jk}^{+}:=a_{jk}-\frac{\delta}{2}$ for $jk\in M_{1}$ , $a_{jk}^{+}:=a_{jk}+\frac{\delta}{2}$ for $jk\in M_{i}$ , $a_{jk}^{+}:=a_{jk}$ for $jk\in M_{\ell}$ where $\ell\in\{2,3\}\setminus\{i\}$ . Since $a_{14},a_{23}\geq\delta$ , we have that $a_{14}^{+},a_{23}^{+}\geq 0$ . Since $M_{i}\subseteq S$ , we also have that $a_{jk}^{+}\leq 1$ for all $jk\in M_{i}$ . Thus $W^{+}$ is indeed a step graphon. Furthermore since every triangle contains at least one element of each $M_{1}$ , it follows that $W^{+}$ is triangle-thin. By Lemma 5.2, we find that $|W^{+}|\leq\frac{1}{4}$ .
Further suppose that $i=2$ . Then
$\displaystyle|W^{+}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot(m_{1}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime}+m_{2}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime})$
$\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{2}(m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime})(m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime})$
$\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{2}=\frac{1}{8}\cdot\varepsilon^{0.32}\cdot(\varepsilon^{0.08})^{2}>36\sqrt{\varepsilon},$
a contradiction, where we used that $m_{3}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime}=m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}\geq m_{1}-m_{2}-6\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geq d_{2}-6\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geq\frac{d_{2}}{2}$ and the last inequality follows since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
So we assume that $i=3$ . Then
$\displaystyle|W^{+}|-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot(m_{1}^{\prime}m_{2}^{\prime}+m_{3}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-m_{1}^{\prime}m_{4}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}m_{3}^{\prime})$
$\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{2}(m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime})(m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime})$
$\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{2}>36\sqrt{\varepsilon},$
a contradiction, where we used that $m_{2}^{\prime}-m_{4}^{\prime}=m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{3}^{\prime}\geq m_{1}^{\prime}-m_{2}^{\prime}\geq\frac{d_{2}}{2}$ as above and the last inequality follows as above.
$\Diamond$
Claim 8.7.6.
$m_{1}-m_{4}<d_{4}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $m_{1}-m_{4}\geq d_{4}$ . By Claim 8.7.5, we have that $m_{1}-m_{2}\leq d_{2}$ . Let $W^{\prime}$ be the step-graphon with $m^{\prime}_{1}=m^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2}=:\overline{m}_{12}$ and $m^{\prime}_{3}=m^{\prime}_{4}=\frac{m_{3}+m_{4}}{2}=:\overline{m}_{34}$ and $a_{ij}^{\prime}:=a_{ij}$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ . Since $m_{1}-m_{2}<d_{2}$ , it follows that $|m_{\ell}-m_{\ell}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{d_{2}}{2}$ for $\ell\in[2]$ . On the other hand, noting that $\overline{m}_{12}+\overline{m}_{34}=\frac{1}{2}$ and therefore that $m_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}-m_{4}^{\prime}$ , then
$|m_{4}-m_{4}^{\prime}|=\bigg|m_{4}+\frac{1}{2}-m_{4}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg|=\bigg|m_{4}+m_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg|\leq\bigg|m_{4}+m_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg|+|m_{1}-m_{1}^{\prime}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{d_{2}}{2}.$
Hence $W$ is $\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{d_{2}}{2}$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ . In addition, we note that $|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|-12(\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{d_{2}}{2})\geq\frac{1}{4}-12\sqrt{\varepsilon}-6d_{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}-18d_{2}$ .
Let $M_{1}:=\{14,23\},M_{2}:=\{24,13\},M_{3}:=\{34,12\}$ . For $i\in[3]$ , let $\overline{a}_{i}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk\in M_{i}}a_{jk}$ . Note that $\overline{a}_{i}\in[0,1]$ . Let $W^{*}$ be defined as $m_{i}^{*}:=\frac{1}{4}$ for all $i\in[4]$ ; for $\ell\in[2]$ and $jk\in M_{\ell}$ , define $a_{jk}^{*}:=\frac{\overline{a}_{\ell}}{\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}}$ ; for $jk\in M_{3}$ , define $a_{jk}^{*}:=1$ . Note that $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal step graphon (specifically it is reflective-cut) and hence $|W^{*}|=\frac{1}{4}$ . Thus $|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|\leq 18\cdot d_{2}$ . On the other hand, again noting that $\overline{m}_{12}+\overline{m}_{34}=\frac{1}{2}$ , we calculate that
$\displaystyle|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|=\frac{1}{4}-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=(\overline{m}_{12}+\overline{m}_{34})^{2}-|W^{\prime}|$
$\displaystyle=(1-a_{12})\overline{m}_{12}^{2}+(2-2(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}))\overline{m}_{12}\overline{m}_{34}+(1-a_{34})\overline{m}_{34}^{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1)\overline{m}_{12}^{2}-2(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1)\overline{m}_{12}\overline{m}_{34}+(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1)\overline{m}_{34}^{2}$
$\displaystyle=(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1)\cdot(\overline{m}_{12}-\overline{m}_{34})^{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq(\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1)\cdot\left(\frac{d_{4}}{2}\right)^{2}$
where for the first inequality we used that for $jk\in M_{3}$ , we have that $1-a_{jk}\geq\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}-1$ by the triangle-thinness of $W$ (applied to the average of two triangles), and for the last inequality we used that $\overline{m}_{12}-\overline{m}_{34}\geq\frac{m_{1}-m_{4}}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot d_{4}$ (since $m_{2}\geq m_{3}$ ). It follows that
$\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}\leq 1+\frac{4}{d_{4}^{2}}\cdot(18d_{2}).$
Substituting this into the second line of the above set of equations, we find that
$|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|\geq(1-a_{12})\overline{m}_{12}^{2}-144\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{d_{4}^{2}}\cdot\overline{m}_{12}\overline{m}_{34}+(1-a_{34})\overline{m}_{34}^{2}.$
Since $\overline{m}_{12}\geq\frac{1}{4}$ and $\overline{m}_{12}\geq\overline{m}_{34}$ , it follows that $1-a_{12}\leq 18d_{2}+\frac{144d_{2}}{d_{4}^{2}}\leq 162\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{d_{4}^{2}}=162\cdot\varepsilon^{0.08}\cdot(\varepsilon^{-0.02})^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ since $\varepsilon$ is small enough. Since $\overline{m}_{34}\geq\frac{b_{3}+b_{4}}{2}\geq\frac{b_{3}}{2}$ , it follows that
$1-a_{34}\leq\frac{1}{\overline{m}_{34}}\cdot\left(18d_{2}+144\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{d_{4}^{2}}\right)\leq 162\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{d_{4}^{2}\cdot b_{3}}\leq 162\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{0.08}}{(\varepsilon^{0.02})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{0.02}}=162\cdot\varepsilon^{0.02}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$
since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
But since $a_{12},a_{34}\leq 1$ , we also have that $|W^{*}|-|W^{\prime}|\geq 2(1-\overline{a}_{1}-\overline{a}_{2})\overline{m}_{12}\overline{m}_{34}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(1-\overline{a}_{1}-\overline{a}_{2})\overline{m}_{34}$ (because $\overline{m}_{12}\geq\frac{1}{4}$ ) and hence
$\overline{a}_{1}+\overline{a}_{2}\geq 1-\frac{36d_{2}}{b_{3}}\geq 1-36\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{0.08}}{\varepsilon^{0.02}}=1-36\cdot\varepsilon^{0.06}.$
It then follows that $W^{\prime}$ is $162\varepsilon^{0.02}$ -close to $W^{*}$ and hence $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
By Claim 8.7.6, we have that $m_{1}-m_{4}<d_{4}$ . Let $W^{\prime}$ be the step-graphon with $m^{\prime}_{i}:=\frac{1}{4}$ for all $i\in[4]$ and $a_{ij}^{\prime}:=a_{ij}$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ . Since $m_{1}-m_{4}<d_{4}$ , it follows that $|m_{i}-m_{i}^{\prime}|\leq d_{4}$ for all $i\in[4]$ and hence that $W$ is $d_{4}$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ . In addition, we note that $|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|-12d_{4}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon-12d_{4}$ .
Let $M_{1}:=\{14,23\},M_{2}:=\{24,13\},M_{3}:=\{34,12\}$ . For $i\in[3]$ , let $\overline{a}_{i}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk\in M_{i}}a_{jk}$ . Note that $\overline{a}_{i}\in[0,1]$ . Note that $|W^{\prime}|=\frac{1}{8}\cdot\sum_{i\in[3]}\overline{a}_{i}$ . Hence $\sum_{i\in[3]}\overline{a}_{i}\geq 2-8\varepsilon-96d_{4}$ . Notice that triangle-thinness implies that for $jk\in M_{i}$ (applied to the average of two triangles), we find that $a_{jk}\leq 2-\sum_{\ell\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{a}_{\ell}$ . Combining the two inequalities above yields that $a_{jk}\leq\overline{a}_{i}+8\varepsilon+96d_{4}$ for $jk\in M_{i}$ . Conversely that also implies (by applying to the other edge of the matching) that $a_{jk}\geq\overline{a}_{i}+8\varepsilon+96d_{4}$ and hence $|a_{jk}-\overline{a}_{i}|\leq 8\varepsilon+96d_{4}$ .
Choose $i\in[3]$ such that $\overline{a}_{i}$ is minimized. Let $W^{*}$ be defined as $m_{i}^{*}:=\frac{1}{4}$ for all $i\in[4]$ ; for $\ell\in[3]\setminus\{i\}$ and $jk\in M_{\ell}$ , define $a_{jk}^{*}:=\overline{a}_{\ell}$ ; for $jk\in M_{i}$ , define $a_{jk}^{*}:=2-\sum_{\ell\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{a}_{\ell}$ . Since $\sum_{i\in[3]}\overline{a}_{i}\geq 2-8\varepsilon-96d_{4}$ , we find by the choice of $i$ that $\sum_{\ell\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{a}_{\ell}\geq\frac{2}{3}\cdot(2-8\varepsilon-96d_{4})\geq 1$ since $8\varepsilon+96d_{4}\leq\frac{1}{2}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough. Hence $W^{*}$ is a step graphon; moreover is triangle-thin by construction. Indeed $W^{*}$ is a value-extremal graphon (specifically it is quad-cut). Finally note that $|\overline{a}_{i}-(2-\sum_{\ell\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{a}_{\ell})|\leq 8\varepsilon+96d_{4}$ and hence for $jk\in M_{i}$ , we have that $|a_{jk}-a_{jk}^{*}|\leq 16\varepsilon+192d_{4}$ . Hence we find that $W^{\prime}$ is $(8\varepsilon+192d_{4})$ -close to $W^{*}$ . But then $W$ is $(8\varepsilon+193d_{4})$ -close to $W^{*}$ ; since $8\varepsilon+193d_{4}=8\varepsilon+193\cdot\varepsilon^{0.02}\leq\varepsilon^{0.01}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, we find that $W$ is $\varepsilon^{0.01}$ -close to $W^{*}$ as desired.
∎
8.2 Full Value Stability
Finally, we formulate our stability version of Lemma 4.29. To that end, we make the following definition.
Definition 8.8.
Let $(H,\varphi)$ be a $[0,1]$ -edge-weighted graph on $n$ vertices. We say $(H,\varphi)$ is $\delta$ -close to a step graphon $(G,\mu,\varphi^{\prime})$ on $n^{\prime}$ vertices if, letting $x_{1},\ldots,x_{n^{\prime}}$ be an enumeration of $V(G)$ , there exists a (not necessarily proper) $n^{\prime}$ -coloring $c$ of $V(H)$ with color classes $(X_{i}:i\in n^{\prime})$ such that $\bigg|\mu(x_{i})-\frac{|X_{i}|}{n}\bigg|\leq\delta$ and at most $\delta n^{2}$ of the pairs $(u,v)$ with $u,v\in V(H)$ satisfy that $|\varphi(uv)-\varphi^{\prime}(x_{c(u)}x_{c(v)})|>\delta$ where we also set $\varphi(uv):=0$ if $uv\not\in E(H)$ and $\varphi^{\prime}(x_{i}x_{i}):=0$ for all $i\in[n^{\prime}]$ .
For the proof, we also need the following somewhat elementary proposition about random variables.
Proposition 8.9.
Let $X$ be a random variable with values in $[0,1]$ . Let $0\leq a<b\leq 1$ . If $\mathbb{P}\left[X\not\in[a,b]\right]\leq p$ , then $\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\in[a-p,b+p]$ and hence $\mathbb{P}\left[~|X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]|>p+(b-a)~\right]\leq p$ .
Proof.
We assume without loss of generality that $\mathbb{P}\left[X\not\in[a,b]\right]=p$ . Let $X_{1}$ be the subset of outcomes of $X$ that lie in $[a,b]$ and $X_{2}$ be the set of outcomes of $X$ that do not, namely those in $[0,a)\cup(b,1]$ . Let $c:=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}\right]$ ; note that $c\in[a,b]$ . Thus $\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[X\in[a,b]~\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[X\not\in[a,b]~\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}\right]=(1-p)c+p\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}\right]=c+p(\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}\right]-c)$ . Since $X$ lies in $[0,1]$ , we have that $\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}\right]\in[0,1]$ and hence $\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}\right]-c\in[-1,+1]$ . Thus $\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\in[a-p,b+p]$ as desired.
∎
We are now prepared to state and prove our stability version of Lemma 4.29.
Lemma 8.10 (Value Stability).
There exists $\varepsilon_{0}>0$ such that for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$ the following holds: If $H$ is a $K_{5}$ -free graph on $n$ vertices, $\varphi$ is a triangle-thin $[0,1]$ -edge-weighting of $H$ , and $|\varphi|\geq\frac{n^{2}}{4}-\varepsilon n^{2}$ , then $(H,\varphi)$ is $\varepsilon^{0.001}$ -close to some value-extremal step graphon.
Proof.
We choose $\varepsilon_{0}$ small enough as needed so that various inequalities throughout the proof regarding $\varepsilon$ hold true. For $v\in V(H)$ , let $d_{\varphi}(v):=\sum_{e\in E(H):v\in e}\varphi(e)$ . Note by hand-shaking that $|\varphi|=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\in V(H)}d_{\varphi}(v)$ .
Let $H_{1}:=H$ . For each $i\in[4]$ , if $V(H_{i})\neq\emptyset$ , choose $v_{i}\in V(H_{i})$ such that $d_{\varphi}(v_{i})$ is maximized and set $X_{i}:=V(H_{i})\setminus N_{H}(v_{i})$ and $H_{i+1}:=H_{i}\setminus X_{i}$ ; if $V(H_{i}):=\emptyset$ , set $X_{i}:=\emptyset$ and $H_{i+1}:=\emptyset$ .
We claim that $V(H_{5})=\emptyset$ . Suppose not, that is there exists $v_{5}\in V(H_{5})$ . But then $v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}$ all exist and hence are pairwise adjacent by construction. But then as $v_{5}\not\in X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X_{3}\cup X_{4}$ , we find that $v_{5}$ is adjacent to all of $v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}$ , contradicting that $H$ is $K_{5}$ -free. This proves the claim. Now let $c$ be the (not necessarily proper) $4$ -coloring of $V(H)$ with color classes $X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4}$ .
Claim 8.10.1.
For each $i\in[4]$ , $\sum_{e\in E(H[X_{i}])}\varphi(e)\leq\varepsilon n^{2}$ .
Proof. Suppose towards a contradiction that the statement does not hold for $i\in[4]$ . Let $s:=\sum_{e\in E(H[x_{i}])}\varphi(e)$ . Hence $s>\varepsilon n^{2}$ .
Define a new edge-weighted graph $(H_{0},\varphi_{0})$ with $V(H_{0}):=V(H)$ ,
$E(H_{0}):=\{xy\in E(H):x,y\in V(H)\setminus X_{i}\}~\cup~\{xy:x\in X_{i},~y\in V(H)\setminus X_{i},~v_{i}y\in E(H)\},$
and
for an edge $xy\in E(H_{0})$ , we define $\varphi_{0}(xy):=\varphi(xy)$ if $x,y\in V(H)\setminus X_{i}$ and $\varphi_{0}(xy):=\varphi(v_{i}y)$ if $x\in X_{i}$ and $y\in V(H)\setminus X_{i}$ (i.e. by replacing vertices of $X_{i}$ by weight-clones of $v_{i}$ ). It follows that $H_{0}$ is $K_{5}$ -free (since $H$ is) and that $\varphi_{0}$ is a triangle-thin $[0,1]$ -edge-weighting of $H_{0}$ (since $\varphi$ is of $H$ ). Hence by Lemma 4.29, we find that $|\varphi_{0}|\leq\frac{n^{2}}{4}$ . Yet
$|\varphi_{0}|=|\varphi|-\bigg(\sum_{v\in X_{i}}d_{\varphi}(v)\bigg)+|X_{i}|\cdot d_{\varphi}(v_{i})+s\geq|\varphi|+s>\frac{n^{2}}{4},$
a contradiction - where for the first inequality we used that $d_{\varphi}(v_{i})\geq d_{\varphi}(v)$ for all $v\in X_{i}$ by the choice of $v_{i}$ .
$\Diamond$
Let $H^{*}$ be the complete $4$ -partite graph with partition classes $X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4}$ and let $\varphi^{*}$ be the $[0,1]$ -edge-weighting of $H^{*}$ where for $e\in E(H^{*})$ , we define $\varphi^{*}(e):=\varphi(e)$ if $e\in E(H)$ and $\varphi^{*}(e):=0$ if $e\not\in E(H)$ . We assume without loss of generality going forward that $|X_{1}|\geq|X_{2}|\geq|X_{3}|\geq|X_{4}|$ (as otherwise we may rename the classes accordingly for the remainder for the proof). By Claim 8.10.1, we find that $|\varphi^{*}|\geq\frac{n^{2}}{4}-5\varepsilon n^{2}$ .
Now we define a step graphon $\overline{W}:=(K_{4},\overline{\mu},\overline{\varphi})$ where $V(K_{4}):=\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}\}$ , $\overline{\mu}(x_{i}):=\frac{|X_{i}|}{n}$ for all $i\in[4]$ and $\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{j}):=\frac{1}{|X_{i}|\cdot|X_{j}|}\cdot\sum_{u\in X_{i},v\in X_{j}}\varphi(uv)$ and for all distinct $i,j\in[4]$ . Note that the ranges of $\overline{\mu}$ and $\overline{\varphi}$ lie in $[0,1]$ by construction. Moreover, $|\overline{W}|=n^{2}\cdot|\varphi_{*}|$ by construction and hence $|\overline{W}|\geq\frac{1}{4}-5\varepsilon$ . Note that $\overline{W}$ is simply the average of all transversal step graphons of $H^{*}$ . Since each of these is triangle-thin by assumption, so is $\overline{W}$ . By Lemma 8.7, we thus find that $\overline{W}$ is $(5\varepsilon)^{0.01}$ -close to a value-extremal step graphon $W$ .
Let $\delta^{\prime}:=\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}$ . We now proceed to show in the remainder of the proof that $H$ is $\delta^{\prime}$ -close to $\overline{W}$ and hence $H$ is $\varepsilon^{0.001}$ -close to $W$ as desired (since $(5\varepsilon)^{0.01}+\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}\leq\varepsilon^{0.001}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough).
To that end, let $B:=\{(u,v)\in V(H):|\varphi(uv)-\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{j})|>\delta^{\prime},u\in X_{i},v\in X_{j}\text{ for some $i,j\in[4]$ }\}$ where we also set $\varphi(uv):=0$ if $uv\not\in E(H)$ and $\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{i}):=0$ for all $i\in[4]$ . Since $\overline{\mu}(x_{i})=\frac{|X_{i}|}{n}$ by construction, to show that $H$ is $\delta^{\prime}$ -close to $\overline{W}$ , it suffices to show that $|B|\leq\delta^{\prime}n^{2}$ . To that end, we define for $i\in[4]$ , $B_{i}:=\{(u,v)\in B:u,v\in X_{i}\}$ , and for $i<j\in[4]$ , $B_{ij}:=\{(u,v)\in B:u\in X_{i},v\in X_{j}\}$ . Hence
$|B|=\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|+2\cdot\sum_{i<j\in[4]}|B_{ij}|$ .
From Claim 8.10.1, we can derive the following upper bound on $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|$ .
Claim 8.10.2.
For each $i\in[4]$ , $|B_{i}|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n^{2}$ and hence $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|\leq 4\sqrt{\varepsilon}n^{2}$ .
Proof. Suppose for a contradiction that $|B_{i}|>\sqrt{\varepsilon}n^{2}$ for some $i\in[4]$ . But then $\sum_{e\in E(H[X_{i}])}\varphi(e)\geq|B_{i}|\cdot\delta^{\prime}\geq\sqrt{\varepsilon}n^{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}>\varepsilon n^{2}$ contradicting Claim 8.10.1, where the last inequality follows since $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Now we proceed with upper bounding $|B_{ij}|$ for all $i<j\in[4]$ to complete the proof. To that end, consider the probability space wherein we choose a vertex $u_{i}\in X_{i}$ uniformly at random (if $X_{i}=\emptyset$ , choose a dummy vertex whose incident edge-weights are zero). Consider the resulting random step graphon $Z:=(K_{4},\overline{\phi},\varphi_{Z})$ where $\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})=\varphi(u_{i}u_{j})$ for $i<j\in[4]$ . Note that $Z$ is triangle-thin as $\overline{W}$ is. Here is a useful claim.
Claim 8.10.3.
$\mathbb{E}\left[~|Z|~\right]=|\overline{W}|$ .
Proof. We calculate that
$\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[~|Z|~\right]$
$\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{k\in[4]}|X_{k}|}\cdot\sum_{u_{1}\in X_{1},u_{2}\in X_{2},u_{3}\in X_{3},u_{4}\in X_{4}}~~\sum_{i<j\in[4]}\varphi(u_{i}u_{j})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{i})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{j})$
$\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{k\in[4]}|X_{k}|}\cdot\sum_{i<j\in[4]}\sum_{u_{i}\in X_{i},u_{j}\in X_{j}}~~\varphi(u_{i}u_{j})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{i})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{j})\prod_{\ell\in[4]\setminus\{i,j\}}|X_{\ell}|$
$\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j\in[4]}\frac{1}{|X_{i}|\cdot|X_{j}|}\cdot\sum_{u_{i}\in X_{i},u_{j}\in X_{j}}~~\varphi(u_{i}u_{j})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{i})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{j})$
$\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j\in[4]}\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{j})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{i})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{j})$
$\displaystyle=|\overline{W}|,$
as desired.
$\Diamond$
Now consider the random variable $d:=\frac{1}{4}-|Z|$ . It follows from Claim 8.10.3 that
$\mathbb{E}\left[d\right]=\frac{1}{4}-|\overline{W}|\leq 5\varepsilon.$
Let $\delta^{\prime\prime}:=(\sqrt{5\varepsilon})^{0.01}$ . Let $\mathcal{Z}$ denote the family of triangle-thin step graphons on $4$ vertices which are $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to some value-extremal step graphon.
Claim 8.10.4.
$\mathbb{P}\left[Z\not\in\mathcal{Z}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Proof. Since $d$ is nonnegative via Lemma 4.23, we have by Markov’s inequality that $\mathbb{P}\left[d\geq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . By Lemma 8.7, if $z\in Z$ is not $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to any value-extremal step graphon, then $|z|<\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ (where we used that $\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ satisfies Lemma 8.7 as $\varepsilon$ is small enough). But then we have $\mathbb{P}\left[Z\not\in\mathcal{Z}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[d\geq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
For $i<j\in[4]$ , let $p_{ij}:=\mathbb{P}\left[|\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})-\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{j})|>\delta^{\prime}\right]$ . It is useful to note that for all $i>j\in[4]$ , we have that $\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})\right]=\overline{\varphi}(x_{i}x_{j})$ . Before proceeding with the main proof, we record the following useful observation.
Claim 8.10.5.
For $i<j\in[4]$ , $|B_{ij}|=p_{ij}\cdot|X_{i}|\cdot|X_{j}|\leq p_{ij}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Proof. Let $k,\ell$ be defined such that $\{i,j,k,\ell\}=[4]$ . The claim then follows since
$p_{ij}=\frac{|B_{ij}|\cdot|X_{k}|\cdot|X_{\ell}|}{|X_{i}|\cdot|X_{j}|\cdot|X_{k}|\cdot|X_{\ell}|}=\frac{|B_{ij}|}{|X_{i}|\cdot|X_{j}|},$
and since $|X_{i}|,|X_{j}|\leq n$ .
$\Diamond$
Now we proceed somewhat along the lines of the proof of Lemma 8.7. First we show that either $\mu(x_{1})$ or $\mu(x_{1})+\mu(x_{4})$ is close to $\frac{1}{2}$ as follows.
Claim 8.10.6.
$|\overline{\mu}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ or $|\overline{\mu}(x_{1})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4})-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ .
Proof. Suppose not. As $|\overline{\mu}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{2}|>\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ , we find that $\overline{\mu}(x_{1})(\overline{\mu}(x_{2})+\overline{\mu}(x_{3})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4}))<\frac{1}{4}-5\varepsilon$ . Similarly as $|\overline{\mu}(x_{1})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4})-\frac{1}{2}|>\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ , we find that $(\overline{\mu}(x_{1})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4}))(\overline{\mu}(x_{2})+\overline{\mu}(x_{3}))<\frac{1}{4}-5\varepsilon$ . But then it follows from Lemma 5.2, that the value of $Z$ is strictly less than $\frac{1}{4}-5\varepsilon$ no matter the choice of $u_{i}$ ; hence $|\overline{W}|=\mathbb{E}\left[~|Z|~\right]<\frac{1}{4}-5\varepsilon$ , a contradiction.
$\Diamond$
We now proceed to case according to how large $\mu(x_{4})$ , $\mu(x_{3})$ , $\mu(x_{1})$ are as follows.
Claim 8.10.7.
$\overline{\mu}(x_{3})>4\delta^{\prime\prime}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $\overline{\mu}(x_{3})\leq 4\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Recall that by Markov’s inequality we have that $\mathbb{P}\left[d\geq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . Let $z\in Z$ such that $|z|>\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . Yet we also calculate that
$|z|\leq\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{2})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{1})\cdot\overline{\mu}(x_{2})+\overline{\mu}(x_{3})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4})\leq\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{2})\cdot\frac{1}{4}+6\delta^{\prime\prime},$
and hence $\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{2})\geq 1-4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}-24\delta^{\prime\prime}.$ Thus it follows that $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})<1-4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}-24\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$ By Proposition 8.9 applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})$ this implies that
$\mathbb{P}\left[\bigg|\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\right]\bigg|>\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+24\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Since $5\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+24\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, it follows that $p_{12}\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ and hence by Claim 8.10.5 that $|B_{12}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ . On the other hand by Claim 8.10.5, we find that $\sum_{i\in[3]}|B_{i4}|\leq|X_{4}|\cdot n\leq 4\delta^{\prime\prime}\cdot n^{2}$ and similarly that $|B_{13}|+|B_{23}|\leq|X_{3}|\cdot n\leq 4\delta^{\prime\prime}\cdot n^{2}$ . Combining, we find that
$\sum_{i<j\in[4]}|B_{ij}|\leq(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+8\delta^{\prime\prime})\cdot n^{2}$ .
Since $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|\leq 4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ by Claim 8.10.2, we find that $|B|\leq 2\cdot(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+8\delta^{\prime\prime})\cdot n^{2}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}\leq\delta^{\prime}\cdot n^{2}$
as desired, where the inequality follows since $2\cdot(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+8(\sqrt{5\varepsilon})^{0.01})+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 8.10.8.
$\overline{\mu}(x_{4})>2\delta^{\prime\prime}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $\overline{\mu}(x_{4})\leq 2\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . By Claim 8.10.7, $\overline{\mu}(x_{3})>4\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Thus for all $z\in Z$ , it follows that $z$ is not $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to a reflective-cut step graphon or a quad-cut graphon. Let $z\in Z\cap\mathcal{Z}$ . Thus we have that $z$ is $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to a star-cut or cycle-cut step graphon. By the definition of star-cut and cycle-cut, we thus find that $\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{2}),\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{3})\geq 1-\delta^{\prime\prime}$ and $\varphi_{z}(x_{2}x_{3})\leq\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Hence $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})<1-\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . By Proposition 8.9 applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})$ this implies that
$\mathbb{P}\left[\bigg|\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\right]\bigg|>\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Since $\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, it follows that $p_{12}\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ and hence by Claim 8.10.5 that $|B_{12}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ . Similar arguments applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{3})$ and $\varphi(x_{2}x_{3})$ imply that $|B_{13}|,|B_{23}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ . On the other hand by Claim 8.10.5, we find that $\sum_{i\in[3]}|B_{i4}|\leq|X_{4}|\cdot n\leq 2\delta^{\prime\prime}\cdot n^{2}$ . Combining, we find that
$\sum_{i<j\in[4]}|B_{ij}|\leq 3\cdot(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime\prime})\cdot n^{2}$ .
Since $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|\leq 4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ by Claim 8.10.2, we find that $|B|\leq 6\cdot(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+2\delta^{\prime\prime})\cdot n^{2}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}\leq\delta^{\prime}\cdot n^{2}$
as desired, where the inequality follows since $6\cdot(\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+2(\sqrt{5\varepsilon})^{0.01})+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 8.10.9.
$\overline{\mu}(x_{1})<\frac{1}{2}-\delta^{\prime\prime}$ .
Proof. Suppose not, that is $\overline{\mu}(x_{1})\geq\frac{1}{2}-\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Yet $\overline{\mu}(x_{4})>2\delta^{\prime\prime}$ by Claim 8.10.8. Hence $\overline{\mu}(x_{1})+\overline{\mu}(x_{4})>\frac{1}{2}+\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Thus for all $z\in Z$ , it follows that $z$ is not $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to any cycle-cut, reflective-cut or quad-cut graphon.
Let $S:=\{i\in\{2,3,4\}:\overline{\mu}(x_{i})>0\}$ . Let $z\in Z\cap\mathcal{Z}$ . Thus we have that $z$ is $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to some star-cut step graphon. By the definition of star-cut, we find that $\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{i})\geq 1-\delta^{\prime\prime}$ for all $i\in S$ and $\varphi_{z}(x_{i}x_{j})$ for all $i<j\in S$ . Hence $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})<1-\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . By Proposition 8.9 applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})$ this implies that
$\mathbb{P}\left[\bigg|\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\right]\bigg|>\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Since $\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, it follows that $p_{12}\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ and hence by Claim 8.10.5 that $|B_{12}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Similar arguments applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})$ for $i<j\in S\cup\{1\}$ imply that $|B_{ij}|\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ . On the other hand for $i\in[4]$ and $j\in[4]\setminus S$ , we have that $|B_{ij}|=0$ .
Combining, we find that
$\sum_{i<j\in[4]}|B_{ij}|\leq 6\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Since $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|\leq 4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ by Claim 8.10.2, we find that $|B|\leq 12\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}\leq\delta^{\prime}\cdot n^{2}$
as desired, where the inequality follows since $12\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
By Claim 8.10.9, we have that $\overline{\mu}(x_{1})<\frac{1}{2}-\delta^{\prime\prime}$ . Thus it follows if $z\in Z$ , then $z$ is not $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to a star-cut step graphon. Hence if $z\in Z\cap\mathcal{Z}$ , we find that $z$ is $\delta^{\prime\prime}$ -close to some cycle-cut, reflective-cut, or quad-cut step graphon. Since each of these are matched, this implies that for all $z\in Z\cap\mathcal{Z}$ , we have that
$|\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\varphi_{z}(x_{3}x_{4})|,|\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{3})-\varphi_{z}(x_{2}x_{4})|,|\varphi_{z}(x_{1}x_{4})-\varphi_{z}(x_{2}x_{3})|\leq 2\delta^{\prime\prime}.$
For $i<j\in[4]$ , let $b_{ij}$ denote a median of $\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})$ . Since $\mathbb{P}\left[|\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\varphi_{Z}(x_{3}x_{4})|>2\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ from above, we find that
$\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{3}x_{4})\geq b_{34}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\leq b_{34}-2\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{3}x_{4})\geq b_{34}\text{ and }\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\leq b_{34}-2\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Since $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{3}x_{4})\geq b_{34}\right]\geq\frac{1}{2}$ by definition of median, we find that $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\leq b_{34}-2\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq 2\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$ A symmetric argument implies that $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\geq b_{34}+2\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq 2\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$ Hence $\mathbb{P}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\not\in[b_{34}-2\delta^{\prime\prime},b_{34}+2\delta^{\prime\prime}]\right]\leq 4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ . By Proposition 8.9 applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})$ this implies that
$\mathbb{P}\left[\bigg|\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{Z}(x_{1}x_{2})\right]\bigg|>4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+4\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]\leq 4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}.$
Since $4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+4\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough, it follows that $p_{12}\leq 4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}$ and hence by Claim 8.10.5 that $|B_{12}|\leq 4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Symmetric arguments applied to $\varphi_{Z}(x_{i}x_{j})$ for $i<j\in[4]$ imply that $|B_{ij}|\leq 4\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ . Combining, we find that
$\sum_{i<j\in[4]}|B_{ij}|\leq 24\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Since $\sum_{i\in[4]}|B_{i}|\leq 4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}$ by Claim 8.10.2, we find that $|B|\leq 48\sqrt{5\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot n^{2}\leq\delta^{\prime}\cdot n^{2}$
as desired, where the inequality follows since $48\sqrt{5\varepsilon}+4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{0.001}}{2}$ as $\varepsilon$ is small enough.
∎
9 The Local Structure
9.1 Value Lemmas
Definition 9.1.
Let $(G,\phi)$ be a nonzero untied edge-weighted graph and let $f=uv$ be a negative edge of $G$ . A vertex $x\in N(u)\cap N(v)$ is said to be obtuse with respect to $f$ if $uvx$ is obtuse; furthermore we say $x$ is $u$ -obtuse with respect to $f$ if $x$ is obtuse and $ux$ is a positive edge of $G$ ; similarly we say $x$ is $v$ -obtuse with respect to $f$ if $x$ is obtuse and $vx$ is a positive edge of $G$ . We say $x$ is acute if $uvx$ is acute. For brevity we omit the with respect to $f$ if $f$ is clear from context.
Lemma 9.2 (Obtuse-Acute Pair Value).
Let $(G,\phi)$ be a nonzero untied triangle-covered edge-weighted graph and let $f=uv$ be a negative edge of $G$ . Let $xy\in E(G[N(u)\cap N(v)])$ . If
$x$ is $u$ -obtuse and $y$ is acute and
(i)
$xy$ is a positive edge, then $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)\leq\textrm{dem}(vy,f)$
(ii)
$xy$ is a negative edge, then $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}\cdot|\phi(f)|+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)$
Proof.
First suppose $xy$ is a positive edge. Then
$\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vxy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vx)=\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,vx)\leq\textrm{dem}(vy,f).$
This proves (i).
So we assume $xy$ is a negative edge. Then
$\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,uxy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,uxy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vxy).$
Since $xy$ is negative, we have that $vxy$ is obtuse. Hence
$\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,vx)=\frac{\textrm{dem}(vy,xy)}{\phi(vy)}+\frac{\textrm{dem}(vy,vx)}{\phi(vy)}=\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(vy)}+\frac{|\phi(vx)|}{\phi(vy)}\leq 1.$
Thus
$\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vxy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vx)=\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot(\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,vx))\leq\textrm{dem}(vy,f).$
Since $uxy$ is acute, we find that
$\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,uxy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,uxy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,xy)=\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(uy,xy).$
We claim the above is at most $\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}\cdot|\phi(f)|$ which will prove (ii) and hence conclude the proof.
To see this, first suppose $uxy$ is sponsored. Further suppose $ux$ is the sponsor of $uxy$ . Then $\textrm{dem}(uy,xy)=0$ , $\textrm{dem}(ux,xy)=\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)}\leq 1$ , $\textrm{dem}(ux,f)=|\phi(f)|$ and the claim follows. So we suppose $uy$ is the sponsor of $uxy$ (and hence $|\phi(uy)|\geq|\phi(ux)|$ and $|\phi(uy)|\geq|\phi(xy)|$ . Then $\textrm{dem}(ux,xy)=0$ , $\textrm{dem}(uy,xy)=\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(uy)}\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}$ , $\textrm{dem}(uy,f)\leq|\phi(f)|$ and the claim follows.
So we assume $uxy$ is not sponsored. Hence $|\phi(xy)|>\phi(ux),\phi(uy)$ . Since $vxy$ is obtuse, we have that $\phi(vy)\geq|\phi(vx)|+|\phi(xy)|$ . This implies that $\phi(vy)>\phi(uy)$ and also that $\phi(vy)>\phi(ux)\geq|\phi(f)|$ . Thus we find that $vy$ is the sponsor of $uvy$ . Hence $\textrm{dem}(uy,f)=0$ . Thus the above is at most $\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)\leq|\phi(f)|\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|\cdot\frac{\phi(ux)}{\phi(ux)+\phi(uy)}}{\phi(ux)}=|\phi(f)|\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)+\phi(uy)}\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}\cdot|\phi(f)|$ as claimed (since $|\phi(xy)|\leq\phi(ux)+\phi(uy)$ as $\phi$ is triangle-covered).
∎
Lemma 9.3 (Extremal Pair Value).
Let $\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{1}{3}]$ . Let $(G,\phi)$ be a nonzero untied triangle-covered edge-weighted graph and let $f=uv$ be a negative edge of $G$ . Let $xy\in E(G[N(u)\cap N(v)])$ . If $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)\geq(2-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , then either
(a)
$x$ is $u$ -obtuse and $y$ is $v$ -obtuse and $|\phi(vx)|,|\phi(uy)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , or
(b)
$x$ is $v$ -obtuse and $y$ is $u$ -obtuse and $|\phi(ux)|,|\phi(vy)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , or
(c)
$xy$ is a negative edge and $|\phi(xy)|\geq(1-3\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|$
Proof.
Let $s:=\frac{\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)}{|\phi(f)|}$ . By assumption $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ . We case according to whether $x$ or $y$ are acute or obtuse as follows.
Case 1: One of $x,y$ is obtuse and one of $x,y$ is acute.
Without loss of generality, we assume $x$ is obtuse and $y$ is acute; similarly without loss of generality we assume $x$ is $u$ -obtuse. First suppose that $xy$ is a positive edge. Then by Lemma 9.2(i), we have that $s\leq\frac{\textrm{dem}(vy,f)}{|\phi(f)|}\leq 1$ , a contradiction. So we assume that $xy$ is a negative edge. Then by Lemma 9.2(ii), we find that
$s\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}+\frac{\textrm{dem}(vy,f)}{|\phi(f)|}\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)},~1\right\}+1.$
Since $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ , we find that
$|\phi(xy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(ux)\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|,$
and hence (c) holds as desired where the final inequality follows since $uvx$ is obtuse and $\phi$ is triangle-covered.
Case 2: Both of $x$ and $y$ are acute.
If $xy$ is a positive edge, then $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=0$ , a contradiction. So we assume $xy$ is a negative edge. Note that
$\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,vy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy).$
Claim 9.3.1.
$\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy),\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,xy),\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,vy),\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy)>0$ .
Proof. Suppose not. We assume without loss of generality that $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vx,xy)=0$ . Now we calculate that
$\displaystyle\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)$
$\displaystyle=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle=\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(uy,xy)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle\leq\phi(ux)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)$
$\displaystyle\leq\phi(ux)\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)}+|\phi(f)|$
$\displaystyle=|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(f)|$
where we used that $\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\leq\phi(ux)$ and that $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(uy,xy)\leq 1$ and that $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)\leq 1$ and that $\textrm{dem}(uv,f)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\leq|\phi(f)|$ and that $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)\leq\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)}$ (since $\textrm{dem}(ux,xy)\leq|\phi(xy)|$ ). Since $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ , it follows
that
$|\phi(xy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|,$
and (c) holds as desired.
$\Diamond$
Claim 9.3.2.
None of $uxy,vxy,uvx,uvy$ are sponsored.
Proof. Suppose not. First suppose that at least one of $uvx$ or $uvy$ is sponsored. We assume without loss of generality that $uvx$ is sponsored and that $ux$ is its sponsor. Thus we find that $\phi(ux)\geq|\phi(f)|$ . But we also have that $\textrm{dem}(vx,f)=0$ and hence $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vx,xy)=\textrm{dem}(vx,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,vy)=0$ , contradicting Claim 9.3.1.
So we assume that at least one of $uxy$ or $vxy$ is sponsored. We assume without loss of generality that $vxy$ is sponsored and that $vy$ is its sponsor. But then we have that $\textrm{dem}(vx,xy)=0$ and hence $\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vx,xy)=\textrm{dem}(vx,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,vy)=0$ , contradicting Claim 9.3.1.
$\Diamond$
Now we calculate that
$\displaystyle\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)$
$\displaystyle=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vx,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(uy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle=\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\textrm{dem}(vx,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,xy)+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(uy,xy)$
$\displaystyle~~~~+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle\leq\phi(ux)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\phi(vx)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vx,xy)+\phi(uy)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(uy,xy)$
$\displaystyle~~~~+\phi(vy)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle=\phi(ux)\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)+\phi(uy)}+\phi(vx)\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(xx)+\phi(vy)}+\phi(uy)\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(ux)+\phi(uy)}$
$\displaystyle~~~~+\phi(vy)\cdot\frac{|\phi(xy)|}{\phi(vx)+\phi(vy)}$
$\displaystyle=2\cdot|\phi(xy)|.$
Since $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ , it follows that $|\phi(xy)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(2-\varepsilon)|\phi(f)|$ and (c) holds as desired.
Case 3: Both of $x$ and $y$ are obtuse.
We assume without loss of generality that $x$ is $u$ -obtuse (since outcomes (a) and (b) are symmetric). If $y$ is $u$ -obtuse, then $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=0$ , a contradiction. So we assume that $y$ is $v$ -obtuse. First suppose that $xy$ is a negative edge. Then we find that
$\displaystyle\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)$
$\displaystyle=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,uy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,xy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vx)$
$\displaystyle=\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,xy)+\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,uy)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,xy)$
$\displaystyle~~~~+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,vx)$
$\displaystyle=|\phi(f)|\cdot\left(\frac{|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(uy)|}{\phi(ux)}\right)+|\phi(f)|\cdot\left(\frac{|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(vx)|}{\phi(vy)}\right).$
Since $\phi$ is triangle-covered, we have that $|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(uy)|\leq\phi(ux)$ and similarly $|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(vx)|\leq\phi(vy)$ . Using this and that $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ , it follows that $|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(uy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(ux)$ and similarly $|\phi(xy)|+|\phi(vx)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(vy)$ . Note that $\phi(ux)\geq|\phi(f)|+|\phi(vx)|$ and $\phi(vy)\geq|\phi(f)|+|\phi(uy)|$ since $\phi$ is triangle-covered. Now if (c) holds, the lemma follows. So we assume (c) does not hold and hence $|\phi(xy)|<(1-3\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|$ . But then we find that
$(1-3\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|+|\phi(uy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(ux)\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot(|\phi(f)|+|\phi(vx)|).$
Rearranging this yields that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq 2\varepsilon\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(vx)|$
and symmetrically by a similar logic we have that
$|\phi(vx)|\geq 2\varepsilon\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(uy)|.$
Combining the two inequalities yields that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq 2\varepsilon\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\bigg(2\varepsilon\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(uy)|\bigg)=(4\varepsilon-2\varepsilon^{2})\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-2\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})\cdot|\phi(uy)|.$
Rearranging once more yields that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq\frac{4\varepsilon-2\varepsilon^{2}}{2\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot|\phi(f)|=2\cdot|\phi(f)|$
and symmetrically we find that $|\phi(vx)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ and hence (a) holds as desired.
So we finally assume that $xy$ is a positive edge. Then we find that
$\displaystyle\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)$
$\displaystyle=\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(ux,uy)+\widehat{\textrm{dem}}_{f}(vy,vx)$
$\displaystyle=\textrm{dem}(ux,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(ux,uy)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\cdot\widehat{\textrm{dem}}(vy,vx)$
$\displaystyle=|\phi(f)|\cdot\left(\frac{|\phi(uy)|}{\phi(ux)}\right)+|\phi(f)|\cdot\left(\frac{|\phi(vx)|}{\phi(vy)}\right).$
Since $s\geq 2-\varepsilon$ , it follows that $|\phi(uy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(ux)$ and similarly $|\phi(vx)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(vy)$ .
Note that $\phi(ux)\geq|\phi(f)|+|\phi(vx)|$ and $\phi(vy)\geq|\phi(f)|+|\phi(uy)|$ since $\phi$ is triangle-covered. Using this, we find that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\phi(ux)\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot(|\phi(f)|+|\phi(vx)|).$
and symmetrically by a similar logic we have that
$|\phi(vx)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot(|\phi(f)|+|\phi(uy)|).$
Combining the two inequalities yields that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\cdot\bigg(|\phi(f)|+|\phi(uy)|)\bigg)=(2-3\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})\cdot|\phi(f)|+(1-2\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})\cdot|\phi(uy)|.$
Rearranging once more yields that
$|\phi(uy)|\geq\frac{2-3\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}}{2\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot|\phi(f)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$
since $2-3\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}\geq 2(2\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{2})$ as $\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{3}$ . Symmetrically we find that $|\phi(vx)|\geq 2\cdot\phi(f)$ and hence (a) holds as desired.
∎
9.2 Extremal Neighborhood Structure Lemma
Definition 9.4.
Let $(G,\phi)$ be a nonzero untied triangle-covered edge-weighted graph on $n$ vertices. A negative edge $f=u_{1}u_{2}$ of $G$ is
•
$\alpha$ -locally-small if for both $i\in\{1,2\}$ , we have that $\bigg|\bigg\{f^{\prime}=u_{i}w\in E_{\phi}^{-}(G):|\phi(f^{\prime})|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|~\bigg\}\bigg|\geq\alpha\cdot n$ ,
•
$\alpha$ -bipartite if there exists a bipartition $\mathcal{P}=(A,B)$ of $N(u_{1})\cap N(u_{2})$ such that at least $(1-\alpha)\cdot\frac{|N(u_{1})\cap N(u_{2})|^{2}}{4}$ of the pairs $(a,b)$ with $a\in A$ and $b\in B$ satisfy that $ab\in E_{\phi}^{-}(G)$ and $|\phi(ab)|\geq(1-\alpha)\cdot|\phi(u_{1}u_{2})|$ .
Lemma 9.5 (Extremal Neighborhood Structure).
There exists $\varepsilon_{0}$ such that the following holds for each $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$ : Let $\alpha\in(0,1)$ . Let $(G,\phi)$ be a nonzero untied triangle-covered edge-weighted graph on $n$ vertices with $\delta(G)\geq(\frac{3}{4}-\varepsilon)n$ and let $f=uv$ be a negative edge of $G$ . If $\sum_{g\in E(G[N(u)\cap N(v)])}\textrm{Val}_{f}(g)\geq(1-\varepsilon)\frac{|N(u)\cap N(v)|^{2}}{2}\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , then $f$ is
(i)
$\alpha$ -locally-small, or
(ii)
$(36\alpha+4900\cdot\varepsilon^{0.0005})$ -bipartite.
Proof.
We choose $\varepsilon_{0}$ small enough as needed to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Suppose not. We assume without loss of generality that $|\phi(f)|=1$ . Note then that $H:=(\textrm{Filter}_{G,\phi}(f),~\frac{1}{2}\cdot\textrm{Val}_{f})$ is a $[0,1]$ -edge-weighted graph; by Lemma 4.31, $H$ is triangle-thin and by Proposition 4.25, $H$ is $K_{5}$ -free. Let $\beta:=\varepsilon^{0.001}$ . Since $\sum_{g\in E(G[N(u)\cap N(v)])}\textrm{Val}_{f}(g)\geq(1-\varepsilon)\frac{|N(u)\cap N(v)|^{2}}{2}\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , we find by Lemma 8.10 that $(\textrm{Filter}_{G,\phi}(f),~\frac{1}{2}\cdot\textrm{Val}_{f})$ is $\beta$ -close to some value-extremal step graphon $W=(K_{4},\mu,\varphi)$ .
Let $S_{u}$ be the set of $u$ -obtuse vertices $x$ in $V(H)$ with $|\phi(vx)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ ; similarly let $S_{v}$ be the set of $v$ -obtuse vertices $v$ in $V(H)$ with $|\phi(uy)|\geq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ . Let $w\in\{u,v\}$ such that $|S_{w}|=\min\{|S_{u}|,~|S_{v}|\}$ and let $S:=S_{w}$ .
Claim 9.5.1.
$|S|\leq\alpha\cdot n$ .
Proof. Suppose not. But then $f$ is $\alpha$ -locally-small and (i) holds, a contradiction.
$\Diamond$
Claim 9.5.2.
Let $\delta:=61\sqrt{\beta}$ . Then $H$ is not $\delta$ -close to a star-cut or cycle-cut step graphon.
Proof. Suppose not. That is, $H$ is $\delta$ -close to a cycle-cut or star-cut step graphon $W^{\prime}$ . Then $H$ has a bipartition $(A,B)$ where $(\frac{1}{2}-3\delta)\cdot v(H)\leq|A|,|B|\leq(\frac{1}{2}+3\delta)\cdot v(H)$ and all but at most $\delta n^{2}$ of the pairs $a\in A,b\in B$ satisfy $\frac{1}{2}\cdot\textrm{Val}_{f}(ab)\geq 1-\delta$ . By Lemma 9.3 (with $\varepsilon$ of that lemma set to $2\delta$ ), each such pair satisfies either Lemma 9.3(a), (b), or (c). Thus the pairs that satisfy (a) or (b) necessarily contain exactly one vertex from $S_{u}$ and one vertex from $S_{v}$ . Since $|S|\leq\alpha\cdot n$ by Claim 9.5.1, we find that there are at most $\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ pairs satisfying (a) or (b). Hence the number of pairs satisfying (c), that is $ab\in E(G)$ and $|\phi(ab)|\geq(1-6\delta)\cdot|\phi(f)|$ , is at least
$\left((\frac{1}{2}-3\delta\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}+3\delta\right)\cdot v(H)^{2}-\delta\cdot n^{2}-\alpha\cdot n^{2}\geq\frac{v(H)^{2}}{4}(1-36\delta-36(\delta+\alpha)),$
where we used that $|N(u)\cap N(v)|\geq n/3$ since $\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{12}$ . It follows that $f$ is $(36\alpha+72\delta)$ -bipartite and (ii) holds (since $4900\sqrt{\beta}\geq 4392\sqrt{\beta}=72\delta$ ), a contradiction.
$\Diamond$
Thus by Claim 9.5.2 we assume $H$ is not $\delta$ -close to a cycle-cut or star-cut step graphon $W^{\prime}$ . It follows that $W$ is reflective-cut or quad-cut.
Let $x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}$ be an enumeration of $V(K_{4})$ such that $\mu(x_{1})\geq\mu(x_{2})\geq\mu(x_{3})\geq\mu(x_{4})$ .
Claim 9.5.3.
Let $b_{4}:=\frac{\delta-\beta}{2}$ and $w_{0}:=\delta-\beta$ . For all $i\in[4]$ , $\mu(x_{i})\geq b_{4}$ . For all $i<j\in[4]$ , $\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})\geq w_{0}$ .
Proof. Suppose not. First suppose there exists $i\in[4]$ with $\mu(x_{i})\leq b_{4}$ . But then in this case we find that $W$ is reflective-cut and $W$ is $(\delta-\beta)$ -close to a a cycle-cut step graphon $W^{\prime}$ and so $H$ is $\delta$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ , contradicting Claim 9.5.2.
So we assume there exist $i<j\in[4]$ such that $\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})\leq\delta-\beta$ . But then $W$ is $(\delta-\beta)$ -close to a cycle-cut step graphon $W^{\prime}$ and hence $H$ is $\delta$ -close to $W^{\prime}$ , contradicting Claim 9.5.2.
$\Diamond$
Let $c$ be the $4$ -coloring of $V(H)$ as guaranteed in the definition of $\beta$ -close and let $V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},V_{4}$ be the color classes (corresponding to the above enumeration $x_{1},\ldots,x_{4}$ ).
Claim 9.5.4.
All of the following hold:
(i)
there exists at most one $i\in[4]$ such that $V_{i}$ has more than $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ $u$ -obtuse vertices,
(ii)
there exists at most one $j\in[4]$ such that $V_{j}$ has more than $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ $v$ -obtuse vertices,
(iii)
there exist at most two $k\in[4]$ such that $V_{k}$ has more than $2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ acute vertices.
Proof. First suppose there exist $i\neq j\in[4]$ such that $V_{i},V_{j}$ each have more than $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ $u$ -obtuse vertices. Let $O_{i}$ be the set of $u$ -obtuse vertices in $V_{i}$ and let $O_{j}$ be the set of $u$ -obtuse vertices in $V_{j}$ . Since $\phi$ is triangle covered we have for each $x\in O_{i},~y\in O_{j}$ that either $xy\not\in E(G)$ or $xy$ is a positive edge (since $xv,yv$ are negative edges as $x$ and $y$ are both $u$ -obtuse) and hence in either case we find that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=0$ . But then each pair satisfies that $|\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)-\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})|>\beta$ (since $\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})\geq w_{0}>\beta$ ); yet there are at least $|O_{i}|\cdot|O_{j}|>\beta\cdot n^{2}$ , contradicting that $H$ is $\beta$ -close to $W$ . This proves (i). A symmetric argument proves (ii).
Finally suppose there exist three distinct indices $i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\in[4]$ such that $V_{i_{1}},V_{i_{2}},V_{i_{3}}$ each have more than $2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ acute vertices. For each $j\in[3]$ , let $A_{i_{j}}$ be a subset of $V_{i_{j}}$ of size $2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ consisting of acute vertices. Let $J$ be the graph with $V(J):=\bigcup_{j\in[3]}V_{i_{j}}$ and $E(J):=\bigcup_{j<j^{\prime}\in[3]}\{xy:x\in V_{i_{j}},y\in V_{i_{j}^{\prime}},xy\in E^{-}_{\phi}(G)\}$ . Since $\phi$ is triangle-covered, we find that $J$ is triangle-free. Hence by Mantel’s Theorem, $e(J)\leq\frac{1}{4}\cdot v(J)^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\cdot(6\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n)^{2}=9\beta\cdot n^{2}$ . but then there exist at least $3\cdot(2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n^{2})-9\beta\cdot n^{2}=3\beta\cdot n^{2}$ $x,y\in V(J)$ with $xy\not\in E(J)$ (that is $xy\not\in E(G)$ or $xy\in E^{+}_{\phi}(G)$ ) and $x\in V_{i_{j}},y\in V_{i_{j}^{\prime}}$ for some $j<j^{\prime}\in[3]$ . But for each such pair we find that that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)=0$ since $x$ and $y$ are acute and $xy\not\in E(G)$ or $xy$ is positive. But then each pair satisfies that $|\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(xy)-\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})|>\beta$ (since $\varphi(x_{i}x_{j})\geq w_{0}>\beta$ ); yet there are at least $|O_{i}|\cdot|O_{j}|>\beta\cdot n^{2}$ , contradicting that $H$ is $\beta$ -close to $W$ . This proves (iii).
$\Diamond$
Claim 9.5.5.
There exists a permutation $\sigma:[4]\rightarrow[4]$ such that all of the following hold:
(i)
all but at most $3\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ vertices of $V_{\sigma(1)}$ are $u$ -obtuse,
(ii)
all but at most $3\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ vertices of $V_{\sigma(2)}$ are $v$ -obtuse,
(iii)
for $i\in\{3,4\}$ , all but at most $2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ of each of $V_{\sigma(i)}$ are acute.
Proof. For each $i\in[4]$ , we have that
$|V_{i}|\geq\mu(x_{i})\cdot v(H)-\beta\cdot n\geq\frac{\delta-\beta}{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon\right)n-\beta\cdot n\geq\frac{\delta-\beta}{2}\cdot\frac{n}{3}-\beta\cdot n>4\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n,$
where we used that $\mu(x_{i})\geq\frac{\delta-\beta}{2}$ , that $v(H)=|N(u)\cap N(v)|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon)n\geq\frac{n}{3}$ that $H$ is $\beta$ -close to $W$ , and that $\delta\geq 7\beta+24\sqrt{\beta}$ . By the pigeonhole principle it follows that for each $i\in[4]$ , $V_{i}$ satisfies at least one of the following: (a) $V_{i}$ has more than $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ $u$ -obtuse vertices; (b) $V_{i}$ has more than $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ $v$ -obtuse vertices; (c) $V_{i}$ has more than $2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ acute vertices.
However by Claim 9.5.4, at most one $i\in[4]$ satisfies (a), at most one $j\in[4]$ satisfies (b), and at most two indices $k,\ell\in[4]$ satisfy (c). Combining these facts, we find that all of these indices exist and are distinct as desired.
$\Diamond$
We say a pair $y\neq z\in V(H)$ is abnormal if $|\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz)-\varphi^{\prime}(x_{c(y)}x_{c(z)})|>\beta$ ; we say the pair is normal if it is not abnormal. Since $H$ is $\beta$ -close to $W$ , we have by definition that there are at most $\beta\cdot v(H)^{2}$ ( $\leq\beta\cdot n^{2}$ ) pairs that are abnormal. We say a vertex $y\in V(H)$ is abnormal if there exist at least $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ vertices $z\in V(H)$ such that $yz$ is abnormal; we say $y$ is normal if it is not abnormal. It follows that there are at most $\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ abnormal vertices of $V(H)$ . Let $\mathcal{N}$ denote the set of normal vertices of $H$ .
Let $\sigma$ be as in Claim 9.5.5. Let $V_{\sigma(1)}^{\prime}$ be the set of vertices in $V_{\sigma(1)}\cap\mathcal{N}$ that are $u$ -obtuse. Let $V_{\sigma(2)}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{N}$ be the set of vertices in $V_{\sigma(2)}$ that are $v$ -obtuse. For $i\in\{3,4\}$ , let $V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}$ be the set of vertices in $V_{\sigma(i)}\cap\mathcal{N}$ that are acute. By Claims 9.5.5 and 9.5.1 and that fact that $|V(H)\setminus\mathcal{N}|\leq\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ , we have for each $i\in[4]$ that
$|V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}|\geq|V_{\sigma(i)}|-4\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n\geq b_{4}\cdot\frac{n}{3}-\beta\cdot n-4\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n\geq 4\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n,$
where the final inequality follows as $\delta\geq 13\beta+48\sqrt{\beta}$ .
Let $J$ be the graph with $V(J):=\bigcup_{i\in[4]}V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}$ and $E(J):=\bigcup_{i<j\in[4]}\{yy^{\prime}:y\in V_{\sigma(i)},~y^{\prime}\in V_{\sigma(j)}^{\prime},~yy^{\prime}\textrm{ is normal}\}$ . Thus $v(J)\geq v(H)-20\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ . Moreover, by the definition of normal, we have that for each $i\neq j\in[4]$ and $y\in V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}$ that $|N_{J}(y)\cap V_{\sigma(j)}^{\prime}|\geq|V_{\sigma(j)^{\prime}}|-\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ .
For $i<j\in[4]$ , define $m_{ij}:=\varphi(x_{\sigma(i)}x_{\sigma(j)})$ . Recall that $m_{ij}\in[w_{0},1]$ . Since $W$ is matched as $W$ is reflective-cut or quad-cut, we have that $m_{12}=m_{34}$ , $m_{13}=m_{24}$ , $m_{14}=m_{23}$ ; similarly we have that $m_{12}+m_{13}+m_{23}=2$ .
Claim 9.5.6.
$m_{13}+m_{23}\leq 1+2\beta$ and hence $m_{12}\geq 1-2\beta$ .
Proof. Since $|V_{\sigma(3)}^{\prime}|>0$ , there exists a vertex $y\in V_{\sigma(3)}^{\prime}$ (that is $y$ is in $V_{\sigma(3)}$ , is normal and acute and not in $S$ ). Since $|V_{\sigma(4)}^{\prime}|>\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ from above, there exists a vertex $y^{\prime}\in V_{\sigma(4)}^{\prime}$ such that $yy^{\prime}$ is normal. Since $|V_{\sigma(1)}^{\prime}|>2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ , there exists a vertex $z\in V_{\sigma(1)}^{\prime}$ such that $yz,y^{\prime}z$ are both normal. Similarly, there exists a vertex $z^{\prime}\in V_{\sigma(2)}^{\prime}$ such that $yz^{\prime},y^{\prime}z^{\prime}$ are both normal.
Since $yy^{\prime}$ is normal, we find that $\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yy^{\prime})\geq\varphi(x_{\sigma(3)}x_{\sigma(4)})-\beta\geq w_{0}-\beta>0$ . Since $y$ and $y^{\prime}$ are both acute, it then follows that $yy^{\prime}$ is a negative edge. Since $\phi$ is triangle-covered, it follows that at most one of $yz,y^{\prime}z$ are negative. We thus assume without loss of generality that $yz$ is a positive edge. Since $y$ is acute and $z$ is $u$ -obtuse, we have by Lemma 9.2(i) that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz)\leq\textrm{dem}(vy,f)$ . Since $y$ is acute and $z^{\prime}$ is $v$ -obtuse by Lemma 9.2(i) and (ii), we also find that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz^{\prime})\leq\min\left\{\frac{|\phi(yz^{\prime})|}{\phi(vz^{\prime})},~1\right\}\cdot|\phi(f)|+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)$ . Since $\textrm{dem}(vy,f)+\textrm{dem}(uv,y)\leq|\phi(f)|\leq 1$ , we find that
$\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz)+\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz^{\prime})\leq 1\cdot|\phi(f)|+\textrm{dem}(uy,f)+\textrm{dem}(vy,f)\leq 2\cdot|\phi(f)|=2.$
Yet since $yz$ is normal we have that $|\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz)-m_{13}|\leq\beta$ ; similarly since $yz^{\prime}$ is normal, we have that $|\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz^{\prime})-m_{23}|\leq\beta$ . Substituting these yields that
$m_{13}+m_{23}\leq\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz)+\beta+\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Val}_{f}(yz^{\prime})+\beta\leq 1+2\beta,$
as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $\mathcal{E}:=\{yz\in E(J)\cap E^{-}_{\phi}(G):|\phi(yz)|\geq(1-(36\alpha+4900\sqrt{\beta}))\cdot|\phi(f)|\}$ .
Claim 9.5.7.
If $w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}\in V(J)$ such that $w_{i}\in V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}$ for each $i\in[4]$ and $J[\{w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}\}]\cong K_{4}$ , then $w_{3}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ ; if $w_{1},w_{2}\not\in S$ , then $w_{1}w_{2}\in\mathcal{E}$ ; and exactly one of the following holds:
(i)
$m_{13}>\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ and $w_{1}w_{3},w_{2}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ ,
(ii)
$m_{23}>\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ and $w_{2}w_{3},w_{1}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ ,
(iii)
$\frac{1}{2}-\beta\leq m_{13},m_{23}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ , $w_{1}w_{3},w_{2}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ and $\textrm{dem}(uw_{3},f),\textrm{dem}(vw_{4},f)\geq 1-6\beta$ , or
(iv)
$\frac{1}{2}-\beta\leq m_{13},m_{23}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ , $w_{2}w_{3},w_{1}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ and $\textrm{dem}(uw_{4},f),\textrm{dem}(vw_{3},f)\geq 1-6\beta$ .
Proof. Note that for each $i<j\in[4]$ , we have that $w_{i}w_{j}\in E(G)$ where this follows since $w_{i}w_{j}$ is normal and hence $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{i}w_{j})\geq 2(m_{ij}-\beta)\geq 2(w_{0}-\beta)>0$ .
Since $m_{34}=m_{12}\geq 1-2\beta$ by Claim 9.5.6, it follows that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{1}w_{2}),\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{3}w_{4})\geq 2-6\beta$ . Suppose that $w_{1},w_{2}\not\in S$ . Then since $w_{1}$ is $u$ -obtuse and $w_{2}$ is $v$ -obtuse, it follows that $\min\{\phi(uw_{1}),\phi(vw_{2})\}<2\cdot|\phi(f)|$ . Hence Lemma 9.3(a) or (b) do not hold for $w_{1}w_{2}$ and thus Lemma 9.3(c) holds for $w_{1}w_{2}$ , that is $w_{1}w_{2}$ is a negative edge and $|\phi(w_{1}w_{2})|\geq(1-18\beta)\cdot|\phi(f)|$ . Thus $w_{1}w_{2}\in\mathcal{E}$ .
Since $w_{3}$ and $w_{4}$ are acute, we similarly find that Lemma 9.3(c) holds for $w_{3}w_{4}$ , that is $w_{3}w_{4}$ is a negative edge and $|\phi(w_{3}w_{4})|\geq(1-18\beta)\cdot|\phi(f)|$ . Thus $w_{3}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ .
Now we first suppose that $m_{13}>\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ thus we find that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{1}w_{3})\geq 2(m_{13}-\beta)>1=|\phi(f)|\geq\textrm{dem}(vw_{3},f)$ . So Lemma 9.2(i) does not hold for $w_{1}w_{3}$ . Hence Lemma 9.2(ii) holds for $w_{1}w_{3}$ , and thus $w_{1}w_{3}$ is a negative edge. By symmetry, we also find that $w_{2}w_{4}$ is a negative edge. Furthermore, we also have that $w_{3}w_{4}$ is a negative edge since $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{3}w_{4})\geq 2(w_{0}-\beta)>0$ . Since $\phi$ is triangle-covered, it follows that $w_{1}w_{4}$ and $w_{2}w_{3}$ are positive edges. By Lemma 9.2(i) since $w_{1}$ is $u$ -obtuse and $w_{4}$ is acute, we find that $\textrm{dem}(vw_{4},f)\geq\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{1}w_{4})\geq 2(m_{14}-\beta)=2(m_{23}-\beta)$ . But then $\textrm{dem}(uw_{4},f)\leq 1-\textrm{dem}(vw_{4},f)=1-2(m_{23}-\beta)$ . By Lemma 9.2(ii) since $w_{2}$ is $v$ -obtuse and $w_{4}$ is acute and $\phi(vw_{2})\geq|\phi(f)|=1$ , we find that
$|\phi(w_{2}w_{4})|\geq\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{2}w_{4})-\textrm{dem}(uw_{4},f)\geq 2(m_{24}-\beta)-(1-2(m_{23}-\beta))\geq 2(m_{23}+m_{24})-4\beta-1\geq 1-4\beta,$
where we used that $m_{23}+m_{24}\geq 2-m_{12}\geq 1$ as $m_{12}\leq 1$ . It follows that $w_{2}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ .
Similarly by Lemma 9.2(i) since $w_{2}$ is $v$ -obtuse and $w_{3}$ is acute, we find that $\textrm{dem}(uw_{3},f)\geq\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{2}w_{3})\geq 2(m_{23}-\beta)$ and hence $\textrm{dem}(vw_{3},f)\leq 1-\textrm{dem}(uw_{3},f)\leq 1-2(m_{23}-\beta)$ . By Lemma 9.2(ii) since $w_{1}$ is $u$ -obtuse and $w_{3}$ is acute and $\phi(uw_{1})\geq|\phi(f)|=1$ , we similarly find that $|\phi(w_{1}w_{3})|\geq 1-4\beta$ and $w_{1}w_{3}\in\mathcal{E}$ . Thus (i) holds as desired.
Next suppose that $m_{23}>\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ . A symmetric argument to the one given above implies that (ii) holds as desired.
So we assume that $m_{13},m_{23}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ . Since $m_{13}+m_{23}\geq 2-m_{12}\geq 1$ , we also find that $m_{13},m_{23}\geq\frac{1}{2}-\beta$ . Thus we find for each $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $j\in\{3,4\}$ that $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{i}w_{j})\in[2m_{ij}-2\beta,2m_{ij}+2\beta]\subseteq[1-3\beta,1+3\beta]$ . Since $\phi$ is triangle-covered, we have that at least one of $w_{1}w_{3}$ or $w_{1}w_{4}$ is positive (since $w_{1}w_{2}$ is negative as already demonstrated).
We assume without loss of generality that $w_{1}w_{3}$ is positive (given the symmetry of outcomes (iii) and (iv)). Since $\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{1}w_{3})\geq 1-6\beta$ and $w_{1}w_{3}$ is positive, we find by Lemma 9.2(i) that $\textrm{dem}(vw_{3},f)\geq\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{1}w_{3})\geq 1-6\beta$ . But then $\textrm{dem}(uw_{3},f)\leq 1-\textrm{dem}(vw_{3},f)\leq 6\beta$ . Since $1-6\beta>6\beta$ as $\beta<\frac{1}{12}$ (since $\varepsilon$ is small enough), we thus find that Lemma 9.2(i) does not hold for $w_{2}w_{3}$ . Thus Lemma 9.2(ii) holds for $w_{2}w_{3}$ ; it follows that $w_{2}w_{3}$ is negative and that $|\phi(w_{2}w_{3})|\geq\textrm{Val}_{f}(w_{2}w_{3})-\textrm{dem}(uw_{3},f)\geq 1-12\beta$ . Since $\alpha\geq 12\beta$ , it follows that $w_{2}w_{3}\in\mathcal{E}$ . Moreover since $w_{2}w_{3}$ is negative and $\phi$ is triangle-covered, we also find that $w_{2}w_{4}$ is positive. Then a symmetric argument to the one given above yields that $\textrm{dem}(uw_{4},f)\geq 1-6\beta$ , $w_{1}w_{4}\in\mathcal{E}$ and (iv) holds as desired (where we note for the reader that outcome (iii) holds if we had assumed $w_{1}w_{4}$ is positive).
$\Diamond$
Since $|V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}|>3\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ and given the degree condition of $J$ mentioned before, it follows that every $xy\in E(J)$ is an edge of some copy of $K_{4}$ of $J$ (and such $K_{4}$ necessarily contains exactly one vertex from each $V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}$ ) and hence Claim 9.5.7 applies to every edge of $J$ .
Note that since $W$ is reflective-cut or quad-cut, we have in either case that $\mu(x_{1})=\mu(x_{2})$ and $\mu(x_{3})=\mu(x_{4})$ and thus $\mu(x_{1})+\mu(x_{3})=\mu(x_{1})+\mu(x_{4})=\mu(x_{2})+\mu(x_{3})=\mu(x_{2})+\mu(x_{4})=\frac{1}{2}$ . Recall that since $H$ is $\beta$ -close to $W$ , we have for each $i\in[4]$ that $\bigg||V_{i}|-\mu(x_{i})\cdot v(H)\bigg|\leq\beta\cdot n$ and hence that $\bigg||V_{\sigma(i)}^{\prime}|-\mu(x_{\sigma(i)})\cdot v(H)\bigg|\leq(\beta+5\sqrt{\beta})\cdot n$ .
Now first suppose that $m_{13}>\frac{1}{2}+\beta$ .
