Thus by Pósa’s theorem there exists a perfect matching $M^{\prime}$ of $H$ ; but then $M\cup M^{\prime}$ combined with the edges incident with $v$ yields a triangle decomposition of $v$ in $G$ using at most $10\varepsilon\cdot n$ interior triangles of $G$ . This proves (b).
∎
As a point of notation as we delve deeper into the proof, we consider a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of a graph $G$ as both a set of triangles (so that $|\mathcal{T}|$ denotes the number of triangles in the packing) and as a subgraph of $G$ (so that $E(\mathcal{T})$ denotes its set of edges $\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}E(T)$ and $\Delta(\mathcal{T})$ denotes its maximum degree that is $\Delta(G[E(\mathcal{T})])$ ).
Here is a useful proposition which shows how to find a still large but small maximum degree triangle packing inside a larger triangle packing.
Proposition 15.8.
Let $G$ be a graph on $n$ vertices and let $d\in(0,1]$ such that $dn\geq 2$ . If $\mathcal{T}$ is a triangle packing of $G$ , then there exists $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{T}$ such that $|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{d}{6}\cdot|\mathcal{T}|$ and $\Delta(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\leq dn+2$ .
Proof.
Let $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{T}$ be chosen such that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\leq dn+2$ and subject to that $|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|$ is maximized. Let $S:=\{v\in V(G):d_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}(v)\geq dn\}$ . Since $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ is maximized, it follows that every triangle in $\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ is contains a vertex of $S$ . Let $\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}$ be the set of triangles in $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ incident that contain a vertex of $S$ . Note that
$|\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}|=\frac{1}{3}\cdot E(\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime})\geq\frac{1}{6}\sum_{v\in S}d_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}(v)\geq|S|\cdot\frac{dn}{6}.$
But $|\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}|\leq|S|\cdot\frac{n}{2}$ . Thus
$\frac{|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|}{|\mathcal{T}|}\geq\frac{|\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}|}{|\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}|}\geq\frac{|S|\cdot(dn/6)}{|S|\cdot n}=\frac{d}{6}$
as desired.
∎
We can now prove our second cleaning lemma which decomposes all of the oddballs while ensuring the leftover graph is cross-divisible as follows.
Lemma 15.9 (Many Oddballs Cleaning Lemma).
For each sufficiently small $\gamma$ the following holds for all large enough $n$ : Let $G$ be a triangle-divisible $2$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices where $\delta(G)\geq(\frac{3}{4}-\gamma)n$ . Suppose that $G$ is $\gamma$ -extremal, $\gamma$ -cross-balanced and contains a triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ consisting only of interior triangles such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq 2\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n^{2}$ . Let
$\mathcal{B}:=\bigg\{v\in V(G):\textrm{IntDeg}_{G}(v)\geq 0.371n\bigg\}.$
If $\mathcal{A}\subseteq V(G)$ such that $\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{A}$ and $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\gamma}n$ and $\mathcal{E}\subseteq E(G\setminus\mathcal{A})$ with $|\mathcal{E}|\leq 2$ , then there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of $G-\mathcal{E}$ that saturates $\mathcal{A}$ such that $G^{\prime}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ is cross-divisible and $\delta(G^{\prime})\geq(\frac{3}{4}-7\sqrt{\gamma})\cdot v(G^{\prime})$ .
Proof.
We choose $\beta$ sufficiently small to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Let $(V_{1},V_{2})$ be the partition of $G$ . Since $G$ is $\gamma$ -extremal, we have by Lemma 15.6(a) that $|V_{i}|\geq(1-\sqrt{\gamma})n/2$ .
Now we turn to cleaning. First, it will behoove us to upper bound $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)$ robustly so that we know we only have to remove a small triangle packing for our surplus reserve.
Claim 15.9.1.
$2\gamma\cdot n^{2}\geq\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)$ .
Proof. Since $G$ is $\gamma$ -extremal, we have that
$e(G)\leq\frac{3}{8}n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\gamma n\cdot\frac{n}{4}+n\cdot\gamma n\right)\leq\left(\frac{3}{8}-\gamma\right)n^{2},$
and
$e_{12}(G)\geq(1-\gamma)\cdot\frac{n^{2}}{4}$
and hence
$\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)=2\cdot e(G)-3\cdot e_{12}(G)\leq 2\gamma\cdot n^{2}$
as desired.
$\Diamond$
Next we show that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)$ is lower bounded linearly by $|\mathcal{B}|$ as follows.
Claim 15.9.2.
$\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)\geq 0.06n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|$ .
Proof. Recall that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)=2\cdot e(G)-3\cdot e_{12}(G)$ . Let $m:=|V_{1}|\cdot|V_{2}|-e_{12}(G)$ (that is the number of ‘missing’ edges in the cut $(V_{1},V_{2})$ ). For each $i\in[2]$ and $v\in V_{i}$ , let $m(v):=|V_{3-i}|-\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G}(v)$ (that is the number of vertices in $V_{3-i}$ non-adjacent to $v$ . By the Handshaking Lemma applied to $\overline{G}$ , we have that $2m=\sum_{v\in v(G)}m(v)$ . Now let $\textrm{Surplus}(v):=d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{m(v)}{2}$ .
Using that $|V_{1}|\cdot|V_{2}|\leq\frac{n^{2}}{4}$ , we thus find that
$\displaystyle\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)$
$\displaystyle=2\cdot e(G)-3(|V_{1}|\cdot|V_{2}|-m)\geq\sum_{v\in v(G)}d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n^{2}+m$
$\displaystyle\geq\sum_{v\in V(G)}\bigg(d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{m(v)}{2}\bigg)=\sum_{v\in V(G)}\textrm{Surplus}(v).$
Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , we have that $d_{G}(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ for all $v\in V(G)$ . Also note that $m(v)\geq 0$ for all $v\in V(G)$ . Combining we find that $\textrm{Surplus}(v)\geq 0$ for all $v\in V(G)$ .
We claim that for each $v\in\mathcal{B}$ , we have that $\textrm{Surplus}(v)\geq 0.06$ . Let $v\in V_{i}$ . But then
$\displaystyle\textrm{Surplus}(v)$
$\displaystyle=d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{|V_{3-i}|-\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G}(v)}{2}=\frac{\textrm{IntDeg}_{G}(v)+d_{G}(v)}{2}-\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{|V_{3-i}|}{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq\frac{.371n+(\frac{3}{4}-\gamma)n}{2}-.75n+\frac{(\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\gamma})n}{2}\geq(0.0605n-2\sqrt{\gamma})n\geq 0.06n,$
as claimed where the last inequality follows since $\gamma$ is small enough.
But then $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)\geq\sum_{v\in V(G)}\textrm{Surplus}(v)\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\textrm{Surplus}(v)\geq 0.06n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
Next we apply Proposition 15.8 to find a large enough subset $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ of $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ with small maximum degree (tied to $\gamma$ ).
Claim 15.9.3.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ of $(G-\mathcal{E})\setminus\mathcal{A}$ consisting only of interior triangles such that $|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|=\lceil\gamma n^{2}\rceil$ and $\Delta(\mathcal{T})\leq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ .
Proof. Let $d:=\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{2}$ . By Proposition 15.8, there exists $\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{0}$ such that $|\mathcal{T}|\geq d\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{2}\cdot 2\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n\geq\gamma\cdot n^{2}$ and $\Delta(\mathcal{T})\leq dn+2\leq\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{2}\cdot n+2\leq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ where the last inequality follows since $n$ is large enough.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{1}:=G-E(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})$ . Let $\varepsilon\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2000}\right]$ be as in Lemma 15.7. Next via our oddball cleaning lemma, Lemma 15.7, we find a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{1}$ of $\mathcal{A}$ (the oddballs) using at most $\frac{1}{6}\cdot 0.06n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|$ (so as to achieve non-negative surplus afterwards).
Claim 15.9.4.
There exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{1}$ of $\mathcal{A}$ in $G_{1}-\mathcal{E}$ using at most $0.01n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|$ interior triangles of $G$ .
Proof. Let $v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}$ be an enumeration of $\mathcal{A}$ . Let $S_{0}:=\emptyset$ . For each $i\in[k]$ ,let $S_{i}:=\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{i}\}$ . We show by induction for $i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}$ that there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}$ of $S_{i}$ in $G_{1}-\mathcal{E}$ using at most $0.01n\cdot|S_{i}\cap\mathcal{B}^{\prime}|$ interior triangles of $G$ . For $i=0$ , the empty decomposition suffices.
So we assume $i\geq 1$ . By induction, there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{i-1}^{\prime}$ of $S_{i-1}$ in $G_{1}-\mathcal{E}$ using at most $0.01n\cdot|S_{i-1}\cap\mathcal{B}|$ interior triangles of $G$ . Let $G_{i}^{\prime}:=(G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{i-1}^{\prime}-\mathcal{E}))\setminus S_{i-1}$ . Let $n^{\prime}:=v(G_{i}^{\prime})$ . Note that $n^{\prime}=n-(i-1)\geq n-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-4\sqrt{\gamma})n$ . Let $(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})$ be the partition of $G_{i}^{\prime}$ given by setting $V_{j}^{\prime}:=V_{j}\cap V(G_{i}^{\prime})$ for each $j\in[2]$ . Recall that $\varepsilon\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2000}\right]$ is as in Lemma 15.7. Since $\gamma$ is small enough, it follows that $|V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq|V_{j}|-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\gamma})n\geq(1-\varepsilon)n/2$ .
Let $\mathcal{A}_{i}:=\{v\in V(G_{i}^{\prime}):\textrm{IntDeg}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)\geq(\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon)n\}$ . Since $\gamma$ is small enough, it follows that $\mathcal{A}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\setminus S_{i-1}$ . Hence $|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\leq|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n\leq\varepsilon\cdot n^{\prime}$ where the last inequality follows since $\gamma$ is small enough.
Finally for each $j\in[2]$ and $v\in V_{j}^{\prime}$ , we have since $G$ is $\gamma$ -cross-balanced that
$d_{j,G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)\leq d_{j,G}(v)\leq d_{3-j,G}(v)+\gamma n\leq d_{3-j,G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)+|\mathcal{A}|+\gamma n\leq d_{3-j,G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)+2\sqrt{\gamma}n+\gamma n\leq d_{3-j,G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)+\varepsilon\cdot n^{\prime},$
where the last inequality follows since $\gamma$ is small enough.
Let $\mathcal{B}_{i}:=\{v\in V(G_{i}^{\prime}):\textrm{IntDeg}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}(v)>(\frac{3}{8}-7\varepsilon)n^{\prime}\}$ . Since $\gamma$ is small enough, it follows that $\mathcal{B}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{B}$ . By Lemma 15.7(i), we have that if $v_{i}\not\in\mathcal{B}_{i}$ , then there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{i}$ of $v_{i}$ in $G_{i}^{\prime}$ using only cross triangles of $G_{i}$ ; similarly by Lemma 15.7(i), we have that if $v_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}$ , then there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{i}$ of $v_{i}$ in $G_{i}^{\prime}$ using at most $10\varepsilon n^{\prime}$ (which is at most $0.01n$ since $\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{1000}$ ) interior triangles of $G_{i}^{\prime}$ . Combining, we find in either case that $\mathcal{T}_{i}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{T}_{i-1}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{i}$ is a triangle decomposition of $S_{i}$ in $G$ using at most $0.01n\cdot|S_{i}\cap\mathcal{B}|$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{2}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}_{1}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Note that
$\textrm{IntSurplus}(G_{2})\geq\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)-6\cdot 0.01n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|\geq 0,$
where the last inequality follows since $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G)\geq.06n\cdot|\mathcal{B}|$ .
Next we find a subset $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $\mathcal{T}$ whose removal will leave a surplus of exactly zero (i.e. cross-divisible) as follows.
Claim 15.9.5.
There exists $\mathcal{T}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{T}$ such that $G_{2}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ is cross-divisible.
Proof. Choose $\mathcal{T}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ such that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G_{2}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})~)\geq 0$ and subject to that $|\mathcal{T}_{2}|$ is maximized. Let $H:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ . If $\textrm{IntSurplus}(H)=0$ , then $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ is as desired. So we assume that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(H)>0$ . Note that $H$ is triangle-divisible since $G$ is triangle-divisible and $H$ is obtained from $G$ by the deleting the triangle packing $\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{T}_{1}$ . Thus by Proposition 15.2 since $G$ is triangle-divisible, we find that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(H)\geq 6$ . Since $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}$ consists of only internal triangles, it follows that $\textrm{IntSurplus}(H)=\textrm{IntSurplus}(G_{2})-6\cdot|\mathcal{T}_{2}|$ . Yet $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G_{2})\leq 2\gamma\cdot n^{2}$ by Claim 15.9.1. Hence
$|\mathcal{T}_{2}|\leq\frac{2\cdot\gamma n^{2}}{6}<\gamma\cdot n^{2}\leq|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|.$
Thus there exists $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{2}$ . Let $\mathcal{T}_{3}:=\mathcal{T}_{2}\cup\{T\}$ . But then $\textrm{IntSurplus}(G_{2}-\mathcal{T}_{3})=\textrm{IntSurplus}(H)-6\geq 0$ , contradicting the choice of $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ .
$\Diamond$
Let $\mathcal{T}:=(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2})$ . Thus $\mathcal{T}$ is a triangle packing of $G-\mathcal{E}$ that saturates $\mathcal{A}$ such that $G^{\prime}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ is cross-divisible and furthermore we find that
$\delta(G^{\prime})\geq\delta(G)-2\cdot|\mathcal{A}|-\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\geq\left(\frac{3}{4}-\gamma\right)n-4\sqrt{\gamma}n-\sqrt{\gamma}n\geq\left(\frac{3}{4}-7\sqrt{\gamma}\right)\cdot v(G^{\prime}),$
where for the last inequality we used that $v(G^{\prime})\geq(1-\gamma)n$ and that $\gamma$ is small enough. It follows that $\mathcal{T}$ is as desired.
∎
15.3 Extremal but at least one side is not close to either $T_{2}(n/2)$ or $\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}$
For this case, to create our reserve of triangles, we also require the (weak) stability version of Turán’s theorem (for just the triangle case) as follows which was proved by Erdős [21, 22] and Simonovits [54].
Theorem 15.10 (Erdős-Simonovits 1966 [23]).
For every $\alpha>0$ , there exists $\varepsilon>0$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $n$ : if $G$ is a graph on $n$ vertices with $e(G)\geq(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon)n^{2}$ and $G$ is not $\alpha$ -close to $T_{2}(n)$ , then $G$ contains a triangle.
We note the following easy corollary of the above theorem.
Corollary 15.11.
For every $\alpha>0$ , there exists $\varepsilon>0$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $n$ : if $G$ is a graph on $n$ vertices with $e(G)\geq(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon)n^{2}$ and $G$ is not $\alpha$ -close to $T_{2}(n)$ , then $G$ contains a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ with $|\mathcal{T}|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Proof.
Let $\varepsilon^{\prime}$ be as in Theorem 15.10 for $\alpha$ and let $\varepsilon:=\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2}$ . Now let $\mathcal{T}$ be a triangle packing of $G$ such that $|\mathcal{T}|$ is maximized. Let $H:=G-E(\mathcal{T})$ . But then
$e(H)\geq e(G)-3\cdot|\mathcal{T}|\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)n^{2}-3\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\cdot n^{2}=\left(\frac{1}{4}-2\varepsilon\right)n^{2}=\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)n^{2}.$
Thus by Theorem 15.10, $H$ contains a triangle $T$ and hence $\mathcal{T}\cup\{T\}$ contradicts the choice of $\mathcal{T}$ .
∎
We are now prepared to prove Theorem 12.5.
Proof of Theorem 12.5.
We choose $\beta$ sufficiently small to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Let $(V_{1},V_{2})$ be a bipartition of $V(G)$ such that $e(G[V_{1},V_{2}])$ is maximized. For the rest of the proof we then consider $G$ as $2$ -partitioned graph with partition $(V_{1},V_{2})$ and hence the $2$ -partitioned graph is also $\beta$ -extremal by the choice of $(V_{1},V_{2})$ . Given the choice of bipartition, it follows that $\textrm{IntDeg}_{G}(v)\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot d_{G}(v)$ for each $v\in V(G)$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}:=\bigg\{v\in V(G):\textrm{IntDeg}_{G}(v)\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\beta}\right)n\bigg\}.$
By Lemma 15.6, we have that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ and $G$ satisfies all of Lemma 15.6(a)-(b). Furthermore, since $\beta$ is small enough and $G$ is not $\alpha$ -close to any of $T_{2}(n/2)+T_{2}(n/2)$ , $\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}+T_{2}(n/2)$ , or $\overline{T_{2}(n/2})+\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}$ , it follows that there exists $i\in[2]$ such that $G[V_{i}]$ is not $\alpha$ -close to either $T_{2}(|V_{i}|)$ or $\overline{T_{2}(|V_{i}|)}$ .
Now we turn to cleaning. First we find our reserve of interior triangles in $G\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . First, via Corollary 15.11, we find a large (but with size still tied to $\beta$ ) triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of interior triangles.
Claim 12.5.1.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $G\setminus\mathcal{A}$ consisting only of interior triangles such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq 2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Proof. For $i\in[2]$ , let $H_{i}:=G[V_{i}]$ . By Lemma 15.6(c), there exists $i\in[2]$ such that $H_{i}$ is not $\frac{\alpha}{3}$ -close to $T_{2}(v(H_{i}))$ . We assume without loss of generality that $H_{1}$ is not $\frac{\alpha}{3}$ -close to $T_{2}(v(H_{1}))$ . By Lemma 15.6(a), we have that $|V_{1}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\beta})n$ and hence
$\delta(H_{1})\geq v(H_{1})-\frac{n}{4}\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{\beta}\right)n\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\beta}\right)v(H_{1}).$
Let $H_{1}^{\prime}:=H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Since $|A|\leq 2\sqrt{\beta}\cdot n$ , we find that $\delta(H_{1}^{\prime})\geq(\frac{1}{2}-6\sqrt{\beta})v(H_{1}^{\prime})$ . Thus
$e(H_{1}^{\prime})\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}-12\sqrt{\beta}\right)\cdot v(H_{1})^{2}.$
Let $\varepsilon$ be as in Corollary 15.11 for $\frac{\alpha}{3}$ . Since $\beta$ is small enough, we have that $12\sqrt{\beta}\leq\varepsilon$ . Thus by Corollary 15.11, there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $H_{1}$ such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\cdot n^{2}$ . Since $\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\geq 2\sqrt{\beta}$ as $\beta$ is small enough and every triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ is interior by construction, the claim follows.
$\Diamond$
Note that $G$ is triangle-divisible. Thus by Lemma 15.9 with $\gamma:=\beta$ and $\mathcal{E}:=\emptyset$ , there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of $G$ that saturates $\mathcal{A}$ such that $G^{\prime}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ is cross-divisible and $\delta(G^{\prime})\geq(\frac{3}{4}-7\sqrt{\beta})\cdot v(G^{\prime})$ .
Finally we apply our idealized decomposition theorem to $G^{\prime}$ , Theorem 14.6 as follows but first we check the conditions. Let $\alpha^{\prime}:=\frac{\alpha}{4}$ . Let $\varepsilon_{0}$ be as in Theorem 14.6 for $\alpha^{\prime}$ .
Let $(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})$ be the bipartition of $G^{\prime}$ given by $V_{i}^{\prime}:=V(G^{\prime})\cap V_{i}$ . Note that $v(G^{\prime})\geq v(G)-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-2\sqrt{\beta})n$ . Given that $G$ satisfies Lemma 15.6(a) and that $\beta$ is small enough, it follows that $G^{\prime}$ satisfies Theorem 14.6(a) for $\varepsilon_{0}$ . Similarly given that $G$ Lemma 15.6(b) and that $\beta$ is small enough, it follows that $G^{\prime}$ satisfies Theorem 14.6(b) for $\varepsilon_{0}$ . Finally given that there exists $i\in[2]$ such that $G[V_{i}]$ is not $\alpha/3$ -close to $T_{2}(|V_{i}|)$ or $\overline{T_{2}(|V_{i}|)}$ , it follows since $\beta$ is small enough that $G^{\prime}$ satisfies Theorem 14.6(c) for $\alpha^{\prime}$ .
Hence by Theorem 14.6, $G^{\prime}$ has an $\mathcal{F}_{0}$ -decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ is a $K_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ as desired.
∎
15.4 More Terminology for Partitioned Graphs
For the next three cases, it will be helpful to recast our notion of closeness away from edit distance and instead look to how partitions of a graph $G$ align with a partitioned family $\mathcal{F}$ . To that end, we first define the concept of a complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup.
Definition 15.12 (Complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup).
Let $\mathcal{F}:=\{F_{1},\ldots F_{k}\}$ be a family of $t$ -partitioned graphs for some integer $t\geq 1$ . For $i\in[t]$ , we let $N_{\mathcal{F}}(i):=\{j\in[t]:~\exists\ell\in[k]\text{ such that }e_{ij}(F_{\ell})\neq 0\}$ . We say a $t$ -partitioned graph $G$ with partition $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{t})$ is a complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup if $G$ is an $\emph{F}$ -blowup and for all $i\in[t]$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}}(i)$ , we have that $e_{ij}(G)=|V_{i}|\cdot|V_{j}|$ . We say $G$ is balanced if $|V_{i}|\leq|V_{j}|\leq|V_{i}|+1$ for all $i<j\in[t]$ . We let $\mathcal{F}(n)$ denote the balanced complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup on $n$ vertices.
In this language, we have that $\mathcal{F}_{1}(n)=\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}+\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}$ , $\mathcal{F}_{2}(n)=\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}+T_{2}(n/2)$ , and $\mathcal{F}_{3}(n)=T_{2}(n/2)+T_{2}(n/2)$ .
Now that we have the concept of a complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup, we can also create a notion of how close a partitioned graph $G$ to a complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup on the same size of parts as follows.
Definition 15.13 ( $\mathcal{F}$ -abnormal Graph).
Let $\mathcal{F}:=\{F_{1},\ldots F_{k}\}$ be a family of $t$ -partitioned graphs for some integer $t\geq 1$ . Let $G$ be a $t$ -partitioned graph with partition $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{t})$ . Let $H$ be the complete $\mathcal{F}$ -blowup with partition $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{t})$ (that is on the same set of vertices of $G$ with the same partition). We say a pair $u\neq v\in V(G)$ is $\mathcal{F}$ -abnormal if either $uv\in E(G)$ but $uv\not\in E(H)$ , or, $uv\not\in E(G)$ but $uv\in E(H)$ . We define the $\mathcal{F}$ -abnormal graph of $G$ , denoted $\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}}(G)$ , as the graph with vertex set $V(G)$ and edge set the abnormal pairs of $G$ . We say a vertex $v\in V(G)$ is $\alpha$ - $\mathcal{F}$ -normal if $d_{\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}}}(v)\leq\alpha\cdot n$ and $\alpha$ - $\mathcal{F}$ -abnormal otherwise.
Finally, we need one last piece of notation.
Definition 15.14.
Partitioning an unpartitioned graph
Let $G$ be a graph. Let $\mathcal{P}=(V_{1},\ldots,V_{t})$ be a partition of $V(G)$ for some integer $t\geq 1$ . Then we let $G[\mathcal{P}]$ denote the $t$ -partitioned graph $G$ with partition $\mathcal{P}$ . Similarly if $H$ is a subgraph of $G$ , we let $H[\mathcal{P}]$ denote the $t$ -partitioned graph $H$ with partition $(V_{i}\cap V(H):i\in[t])$ .
15.5 Close to $\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}+\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}$
We now proceed to the next major case. For this and the next major case, we need an easy proposition to construct our reserve set of triangles as follows.
Proposition 15.15.
If $G$ is a graph on $n$ vertices with $e(G)\geq\frac{n^{2}}{4}$ , then there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $G$ with $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{e(G)-(n^{2}/4)}{3}$ .
Proof.
Let $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ be a triangle packing of $G$ such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|$ is maximized. Let $H:=G-E(\mathcal{T}_{0})$ . Since $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|$ is maximized, it follows that $H$ is triangle-free and hence by Turán’s theorem, we have that $e(H)\leq\frac{n^{2}}{4}$ . Thus
$|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{e(G)-e(H)}{3}\geq\frac{e(G)-(n^{2}/4)}{3}$
as desired.
∎
For our next three cases, we also need a lemma about when it is possible to decompose a set of interior edges $\mathcal{O}$ of a $2$ -partitioned graph that is of low maximum degree via disjoint cross triangle also of relatively low maximum degree, but first a definition.
Definition 15.16.
Let $G$ be a graph and let $\mathcal{O}\subseteq E(G)$ . A triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of $G$ is $\mathcal{O}$ -disjoint if $|\mathcal{O}\cap E(T)|\leq 1$ for all $T\in\mathcal{T}$ . We say $\mathcal{T}$ is $\mathcal{O}$ -encompassed if every $T\in\mathcal{T}$ contains exactly one edge of $\mathcal{O}$ and every edge of $\mathcal{O}$ is in a triangle of $\mathcal{T}$ .
Lemma 15.17.
There exists $\gamma_{0}$ such that the following holds for all $\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{0}]$ and large enough $n$ : Let $G$ be a $2$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices. If $\mathcal{O}\subseteq E_{11}(G)\cup E_{22}(G)$ with $\Delta(\mathcal{O})\leq\gamma\cdot n$ and for each $i\in[2]$ and $uv\in\mathcal{O}\cap E_{ii}(G)$ we have that $|N_{3-i}(u)\cap N_{3-i}(v)|\geq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ , then there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of $G$ such that all of the following hold:
(i)
$\mathcal{T}$ consists only of cross triangles of $G$ ,
(ii)
$\mathcal{T}$ is $\mathcal{O}$ -encompassed, and
(iii)
$\Delta(\mathcal{T})\leq 4\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ .
Proof.
Let $\mathcal{T}$ be a triangle-packing of $G$ consisting only of cross triangles of $G$ such that every triangle in $\mathcal{T}$ contains exactly one edge of $\mathcal{O}$ and that for every $v\in V(G)$ , $|\{vwx\in\mathcal{T}:wx\in\mathcal{O}\}|\leq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ , and subject to those conditions $|\mathcal{T}|$ is maximized. Let $\mathcal{O}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{O}\cap E(\mathcal{T})$ . If $|\mathcal{O}^{\prime}|=|\mathcal{O}|$ , then $\mathcal{T}$ is as desired since $\Delta(\mathcal{T})\leq 4\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ .
So we assume that $|\mathcal{O}^{\prime}|<|\mathcal{O}|$ . Thus there exists $f=xy\in\mathcal{O}\setminus E(\mathcal{T}^{\prime})$ . We assume without loss of generality that $x\in V_{1}$ and $y\in V_{1}$ . By assumption $|N(x)\cap N(y)\cap V_{2}|\geq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n$ . Let $W:=\{v\in V(G):|\{vwx\in\mathcal{T}:wx\in\mathcal{O}\}|>\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n-1\}$ . It follows that
$|\mathcal{T}|\geq(\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n-1)\cdot|W|\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n\cdot|W|,$
where the last inequality follows since $\gamma$ is small enough and $n$ is large enough. Yet $|\mathcal{T}|<|\mathcal{O}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot n^{2}$ . Hence
$|W|<\frac{\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot n^{2}}{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n}=\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n,$
and hence $N(x)\cap N(y)\cap V_{2}\setminus W\neq\emptyset$ . Thus there exists $z\in N(x)\cap N(y)\cap V_{2}\setminus W$ . But then $\mathcal{T}\cup\{xyz\}$ contradicts the choice of $\mathcal{T}$ .
∎
Next we need the notion of a parity fixer defined as follows. This particular definition is specific to our problem but the same gadget works for both $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ and $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ (which seems to be mostly happenstance).
Definition 15.18.
Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph. A parity-fixer for $G$ is subgraph $F$ of $G$ such that $V(F)=\{v,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\}$ such that $vu_{i}\in E(G)$ for all $i\in[4]$ and $u_{1}u_{3}u_{2}u_{4}$ is a cycle in $G$ , and for each $i\in[4]$ , $\mathcal{P}_{G}(u_{i})=i$ . We call $v$ the center of the fixer and $u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ the spokes.
We are now prepared to prove Theorem 12.6.
Proof of Theorem 12.6.
We choose $\alpha$ sufficiently small to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Since $G$ is $\alpha$ -close to $\mathcal{F}_{1}(n)$ , there exists a partition $\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=(V_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,V_{4}^{\prime})$ of $V(G)$ such that for each $i\in[4]$ , we have $\lfloor n/4\rfloor\leq|V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\lceil n/4\rceil$ and $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(G[\mathcal{P^{\prime}}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}:=\bigg\{v\in V(G):d_{\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}])}(v)\geq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\bigg\}.$
Since $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ , it follows that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ .
Let $L:=\{v\in\mathcal{A}:|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\}$ and $R:=\mathcal{A}\setminus L(\mathcal{A})$ . Let $\mathcal{A}_{1}:=\{v\in L:|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{2}^{\prime}|\}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{2}:=L\setminus\mathcal{A}_{1}$ ; similarly let $\mathcal{A}_{3}:=\{v\in R:|N(v)\cap V_{3}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{4}^{\prime}|\}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{4}:=R\setminus\mathcal{A}_{3}$ . For each $i\in[4]$ , let $V_{i}:=(V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A})\cup\mathcal{A}_{i}$ . Let $\mathcal{P}:=(V_{i}:i\in[4])$ . Note $\mathcal{P}$ is a partition of $V(G)$ .
Claim 12.6.1.
$G[\mathcal{P}]$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced.
Proof. For each $i\in[4]$ , we have that $|V_{i}|\geq|V_{i}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{A}|\geq\frac{n}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and the claim follows.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.6.2.
Let $i\in[4]$ , $v\in V_{i}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(i)$ . If $v\notin\mathcal{A}$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ ; if $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{10}$ .
Proof. First suppose $v\not\in\mathcal{A}$ . Hence $|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq|V_{j}^{\prime}|-\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n=(\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . But then $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{\alpha})n-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ as desired.
So we assume $v\in\mathcal{A}$ . We assume without loss of generality that $i=1$ and hence $v\in\mathcal{A}_{1}$ . Then by definition of $\mathcal{A}_{1}$ , we have that $|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|$ and $|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{2}^{\prime}|$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , it follows that $|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq\frac{3}{8}n$ . First suppose $j\in\{3,4\}$ . But then we have $|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\geq\frac{n}{10}$ where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. So we assume $j=1$ . Note that $|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\geq d_{G}(v)-|V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ . Thus $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot\geq\frac{n}{10}$ where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.6.3.
There exists a parity-fixer for $G[\mathcal{P}]$ whose spokes are in $V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ .
Proof. Recall that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . By Claim .1, we have that $|V_{i}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ for each $i\in[2]$ . Recall that by Claim .2, we have that for all $i\in[4]$ , $w\in V_{i}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(i)$ that $|N_{G}(w)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ (a fact we used extensively and implicitly in what follows).
Since $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{4})$ is a partition of $V(G)$ , there exists $i\in[4]$ such that $|V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ . Without loss of generality we assume that $|V_{1}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ . Since $\alpha$ is small enough, it follows that $|V_{2}\setminus\mathcal{A}|>0$ . Let $u_{2}\in V_{2}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3n}{4}$ and $|V_{1}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ , it follows that $|N(u_{2})\cap V_{1}|>0$ . Let $v\in N(u_{2})\cap V_{1}$ . By Claim .2, we have that $|N(v)\cap V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{10}$ for all $i\in[4]\setminus\{2\}$ . Thus there exists $u_{3}\in V_{3}\cap N(u_{2})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Similarly it follows from Claim .2 that there exists $u_{4}\in V_{4}\cap N(u_{2})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Finally by Claim .2, there exists $u_{1}\in V_{1}\cap N(u_{3})\cap N(u_{4})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . But then $v,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ form a parity-fixer all of whose spokes are in $V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
By Claim .3, there exists a parity-fixer $F$ for $G$ with center $v$ and spokes $u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ where $u_{i}\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ for each $i\in[4]$ . Let $\mathcal{Q}_{1}:=\{vu_{1}u_{3},vu_{2}u_{4}\}$ and let $\mathcal{E}=\{u_{1}u_{4},u_{2}u_{3}\}$ . Note that $E(F)=\mathcal{Q}_{1}\cup\mathcal{E}$ and also note that $\mathcal{E}\subseteq E(G\setminus\mathcal{A})$ . Let $G_{0}:=G-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})$ .
Now we turn to cleaning. Let $\mathcal{P}^{*}$ be the partition $(V_{1}\cup V_{2},V_{3}\cup V_{4})$ of $V(G)$ .
Claim 12.6.4.
$G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $3\sqrt{\alpha}$ -cross-balanced.
Proof. For each $v\in V(G_{0})$ , we find that $\textrm{IntDeg}_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]}(v)\leq\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G}(v)+|\mathcal{A}|+2\leq\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]}(v)+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n+2$ and hence $G^{*}$ is $\sqrt{\alpha}$ -cross-balanced since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.6.5.
$G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $15\sqrt{\alpha}$ -extremal.
Proof. Note that $|V_{1}^{*}|=|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\alpha})n$ and similarly $|V_{2}^{*}|=|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . For each $v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)$ , we have by Claim .2 that $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . Thus
$e_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P^{*}}]}(V_{1}^{*},V_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(n-|\mathcal{A}|)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-6\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n-2\geq(1-15\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{n^{2}}{4},$
where for the last inequality we used that $\alpha$ is small enough. On the other hand, for each $i\in[4]$ and $v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ , we also find that
$d_{G_{0}}(v)\leq d_{G}(v)\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n+\sum_{j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)}|V_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\left(\frac{3}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n$
and hence $G[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $15\sqrt{\alpha}$ -extremal.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.6.6.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $(G_{0}-\mathcal{E})\setminus\mathcal{A}$ consisting only of interior triangles such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq 30\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Proof. Let $H:=G[V_{1}]$ . By definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , we find that if $v\in V(H)\cap\mathcal{A}$ , then
$d_{H}(v)\geq v(H)-\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\geq\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n}{v(H)}\right)\cdot v(H)\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{1-8\sqrt{\alpha}}\right)\geq(1-5\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(H),$
where we used that $v(H)\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})$ and $\alpha$ is small enough. Since $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ , it follows that
$e(H)\geq\frac{1}{2}(v(H)-2\sqrt{\alpha}n)\cdot(v(H)-\sqrt{\alpha}n)\geq(1-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(H)^{2}}{2}.$
Thus by Proposition 15.15, there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $G[V_{1}]$ with
$|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{e(G[V_{1}])-\frac{v(H)^{2}}{4}}{3}\geq\frac{(1-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(H)^{2}}{2}-\frac{v(H)^{2}}{4}}{3}\geq\left(\frac{1}{12}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)\cdot v(H)^{2}\geq 30\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n^{2},$
as desired where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Thus by Lemma 15.9 applied to $G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ with $\gamma:=15\sqrt{\alpha}$ , there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{1}$ of $G_{0}-\mathcal{E}$ that saturates $\mathcal{A}$ in $G_{0}$ such that letting $G_{1}:=(G_{0}-E(\mathcal{T}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ , we have that $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible and $\delta(G_{1})\geq(\frac{3}{4}-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(G_{1})$ .
Claim 12.6.7.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $G_{1}-\mathcal{E}$ with $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 8\sqrt{\alpha}n$ such that letting $G_{2}:=G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ , we have that $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup and $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible.
Proof. Let $\mathcal{O}:=\{e\in E(G_{1}):e\in E(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(G_{1}[\mathcal{P}]))\}$ . Note that $\Delta(\mathcal{O})\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ by definition of $\mathcal{A}$ . Moreover $|V_{i}\cap V(G_{1})|\geq|V_{i}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-2\sqrt{\alpha})n\geq(1-4\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(G_{1})$ . Thus $G_{1}$ is $4\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced. Let $\gamma:=4\sqrt{\alpha}$ . For each $i\in[2]$ and $uv\in\mathcal{O}\cap E_{ii}(G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}])$ , we find by Claim .2 that
$|N_{G_{1}}(u)\cap N_{G_{1}}(v)\cap V_{3-i}^{*}|\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-6\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n\geq(1-8\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(G_{1})}{2}\geq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot v(G_{1}),$
where the final inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. Hence by Lemma 15.17, there exists a triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ satisfying Lemma 15.17(i)-(iii). Condition (i) says that $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ consists only of cross triangles of $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ and hence $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible since $G_{1}[P^{*}]$ is. Condition (ii) yields that $\mathcal{O}\subseteq E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ and hence $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup. Condition (iii) gives that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 4\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n\leq 8\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . Hence $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{2}:=G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ . Next we utilize our parity-fixer. Let $\mathcal{Q}_{2}:=\{vu_{1}u_{4},vu_{2}u_{3}\}$ . Note given our construction, we have $(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2})\cap\mathcal{E}=\emptyset$ and hence $\mathcal{E}\subseteq E(G_{2})$ .
Claim 12.6.8.
Either $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ or $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -divisible.
Proof. First suppose $e_{ij}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is even for all $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $j\in\{3,4\}$ . Then $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -divisible by Lemma 13.19 as desired.
So we assume there exists $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $j\in\{3,4\}$ such that $e_{ij}[G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is odd. Since $G_{2}$ is Eulerian and $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup, it follows that for each $i\in[2]$ that $\sum_{j\in\{3,4\}}e_{ij}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is even and similarly for each $j\in\{3,4\}$ that $\sum_{i\in[2]}e_{ij}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is even. But then it follows that for all $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $j\in\{3,4\}$ , we have that $e_{ij}[G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is odd.
Thus we let $G_{2}^{\prime}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ . But then that $e_{13}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-1$ , $e_{14}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+1,~e_{23}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{23}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+1,~e_{24}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{24}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-1$ are all even and hence by Lemma 13.19, we find that $G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -divisible as desired.
$\Diamond$
If $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -divisible, let $G_{3}:=G_{2}$ and $\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{1}$ ; otherwise let $G_{3}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ and $\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{2}$ . Finally we apply our idealized decomposition theorem, Theorem 14.7, to $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ as follows but first we check the conditions. Let $\varepsilon_{1}$ be as in Theorem 14.7 for $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ .
Note that $v(G_{3})\geq v(G)-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . Given that $G[\mathcal{P}]$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced and that $\alpha$ is small enough, it follows that $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ is $\varepsilon_{1}$ -nearly-balanced. Similarly given the definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , it follows $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\varepsilon_{1}$ -nearly-complete $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup.
Hence by Theorem 14.7, $G_{3}$ has an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ is a $K_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ as desired.
∎
15.6 Close to $\overline{T_{2}(n/2)}+T_{2}(n/2)$
We are now prepared to prove Theorem 12.7.
Proof of Theorem 12.7.
We choose $\alpha$ sufficiently small to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Since $G$ is $\alpha$ -close to $\mathcal{F}_{2}(n)$ , there exists a partition $\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=(V_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,V_{4}^{\prime})$ of $V(G)$ such that for each $i\in[4]$ , we have $\lfloor n/4\rfloor\leq|V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\lceil n/4\rceil$ and $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(G[\mathcal{P^{\prime}}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}:=\bigg\{v\in V(G):d_{\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}])}(v)\geq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\bigg\}.$
Since $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ , it follows that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ .
Let $L:=\{v\in\mathcal{A}:|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\}$ and $R:=\mathcal{A}\setminus L(\mathcal{A})$ . Let $\mathcal{A}_{1}:=\{v\in L:|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{2}^{\prime}|\}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{2}:=L\setminus\mathcal{A}_{1}$ ; similarly let $\mathcal{A}_{3}:=\{v\in R:|N(v)\cap V_{4}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{3}^{\prime}|\}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{4}:=R\setminus\mathcal{A}_{3}$ . For each $i\in[4]$ , let $V_{i}:=(V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A})\cup\mathcal{A}_{i}$ . Let $\mathcal{P}:=(V_{i}:i\in[4])$ . Note $\mathcal{P}$ is a partition of $V(G)$ .
Claim 12.7.1.
$G[\mathcal{P}]$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced.
Proof. For each $i\in[4]$ , we have that $|V_{i}|\geq|V_{i}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{A}|\geq\frac{n}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and the claim follows.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.7.2.
Let $i\in[4]$ , $v\in V_{i}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(i)$ . If $v\notin\mathcal{A}$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ ; if $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{10}$ .
Proof. First suppose $v\not\in\mathcal{A}$ . Hence $|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq|V_{j}^{\prime}|-\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n=(\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . But then $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-\sqrt{\alpha})n-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ as desired.
So we assume $v\in\mathcal{A}$ . First suppose that $i\in\{1,2\}$ . We assume without loss of generality that $i=1$ and hence $v\in\mathcal{A}_{1}$ . Then by definition of $\mathcal{A}_{1}$ , we have that $|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|$ and $|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{2}^{\prime}|$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , it follows that $|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq\frac{3}{8}n$ . First suppose $j\in\{3,4\}$ . But then we have $|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\geq\frac{n}{10}$ where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. So we assume $j=1$ . Note that $|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\geq d_{G}(v)-|V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{1}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ . Thus $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot\geq\frac{n}{10}$ as desired where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
So we assume that $i\in\{3,4\}$ . We assume without loss of generality that $i=3$ and hence $v\in\mathcal{A}_{3}$ . Then by definition of $\mathcal{A}_{3}$ , we have that $|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\geq|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|$ and $|N(v)\cap V_{4}^{\prime}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{3}^{\prime}|$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , it follows that $|N(v)\cap(V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime})|\geq\frac{3}{8}n$ . First suppose $j\in\{1,2\}$ . But then we have $|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\geq\frac{n}{10}$ where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. So we assume $j=4$ . Note that $|N(v)\cap(V_{3}^{\prime}\cup V_{4}^{\prime})|\geq d_{G}(v)-|V_{1}^{\prime}\cup V_{2}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ and hence $|N(v)\cap V_{4}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{8}$ . Thus $|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|-|V_{j}^{\prime}\setminus V_{j}|\geq\frac{n}{8}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot\geq\frac{n}{10}$ as desired where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.7.3.
There exists a parity-fixer for $G[\mathcal{P}]$ whose spokes are in $V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ .
Proof. Recall that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . By Claim .1, we have that $|V_{i}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ for each $i\in[2]$ . Recall that by Claim .2, we have that for all $i\in[4]$ , $w\in V_{i}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(i)$ that $|N_{G}(w)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ (a fact we used extensively and implicitly in what follows).
Since $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{4})$ is a partition of $V(G)$ , there exists $i\in[4]$ such that $|V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ . First suppose that there exists $i\in[2]$ such that $|V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ . We assume without loss of generality that $|V_{1}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ . Since $\alpha_{0}$ is small enough, it follows that $|V\setminus\mathcal{A}|>0$ . Let $u_{2}\in V_{2}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3n}{4}$ and $|V_{1}|\geq\frac{n}{4}$ and $\alpha$ is small enough, it follows that $|N(u_{2})\cap V_{1}|>0$ . Let $v\in N(u_{2})\cap V_{1}$ . By Claim .2, we have that $|N(v)\cap V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{10}$ for all $i\in[4]\setminus\{2\}$ . Thus there exists $u_{3}\in V_{3}\cap N(u_{2})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Similarly it follows from Claim .2 that there exists $u_{4}\in V_{4}\cap N(u_{2})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Finally by Claim .2, there exists $u_{1}\in V_{1}\cap N(u_{3})\cap N(u_{4})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . But then $v,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ form a parity-fixer all of whose spokes are in $V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ as desired.
So we assume that $|V_{1}|,|V_{2}|<\frac{n}{4}$ . It follows that there exists $i\in\{3,4\}$ such that $|V_{i}|>\frac{n}{4}$ . Without loss of generality we assume that $|V_{3}|>\frac{n}{4}$ . Since $\alpha_{0}$ is small enough, it follows that $|V_{3}\setminus\mathcal{A}|>0$ . Let $u_{3}\in V_{2}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3n}{4}$ and $|V_{3}|>\frac{n}{4}$ , it follows that $|N(u_{3})\cap V_{3}|>0$ . Let $v\in N(u_{3})\cap V_{3}$ . By Claim .2, we have that $|N(v)\cap V_{i}|\geq\frac{n}{10}$ for all $i\in[4]\setminus\{3\}$ . Thus there exists $u_{2}\in V_{2}\cap N(u_{3})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Similarly it follows from Claim .2 that there exists $u_{1}\in V_{1}\cap N(u_{3})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Finally by Claim .2, there exists $u_{4}\in V_{4}\cap N(u_{1})\cap N(u_{2})\cap N(v)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . But then $v,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ form a parity-fixer all of whose spokes are in $V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
By Claim .3, there exists a parity-fixer $F$ for $G$ with center $v$ and spokes $u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}$ where $u_{i}\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ for each $i\in[4]$ . Let $\mathcal{Q}_{1}:=\{vu_{1}u_{3},vu_{2}u_{4}\}$ and let $\mathcal{E}=\{u_{1}u_{4},u_{2}u_{3}\}$ . Note that $E(F)=\mathcal{Q}_{1}\cup\mathcal{E}$ and also note that $\mathcal{E}\subseteq E(G\setminus\mathcal{A})$ . Let $G_{0}:=G-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})$ .
Now we turn to cleaning. Let $\mathcal{P}^{*}$ be the partition $(V_{1}\cup V_{2},V_{3}\cup V_{4})$ of $V(G)$ .
Claim 12.7.4.
$G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $3\sqrt{\alpha}$ -cross-balanced.
Proof. For each $v\in V(G_{0})$ , we find that $\textrm{IntDeg}_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]}(v)\leq\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G}(v)+|\mathcal{A}|+2\leq\textrm{CrossDeg}_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]}(v)+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n+2$ and hence $G^{*}$ is $\sqrt{\alpha}$ -cross-balanced since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.7.5.
$G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $15\sqrt{\alpha}$ -extremal.
Proof. Note that $|V_{1}^{*}|=|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\alpha})n$ and similarly $|V_{2}^{*}|=|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|\geq(\frac{1}{2}-4\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . For each $v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)$ , we have by Claim .2 that $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-3\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . Thus
$e_{G_{0}[\mathcal{P^{*}}]}(V_{1}^{*},V_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot(n-|\mathcal{A}|)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-6\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n-2\geq(1-15\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{n^{2}}{4},$
where for the last inequality we used that $\alpha$ is small enough. On the other hand, for each $i\in[4]$ and $v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ , we also find that
$d_{G_{0}}(v)\leq d_{G}(v)\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n+\sum_{j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)}|V_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\left(\frac{3}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n$
and hence $G[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is $15\sqrt{\alpha}$ -extremal.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.7.6.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $(G_{0}-\mathcal{E})\setminus\mathcal{A}$ consisting only of interior triangles such that $|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq 30\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n^{2}$ .
Proof. Let $H:=G[V_{1}]$ . By definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , we find that if $v\in V(H)\cap\mathcal{A}$ , then
$d_{H}(v)\geq v(H)-\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\geq\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n}{v(H)}\right)\cdot v(H)\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\alpha}}{1-8\sqrt{\alpha}}\right)\geq(1-5\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(H),$
where we used that $v(H)\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})$ and $\alpha$ is small enough. Since $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ , it follows that
$e(H)\geq\frac{1}{2}(v(H)-2\sqrt{\alpha}n)\cdot(v(H)-\sqrt{\alpha}n)\geq(1-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(H)^{2}}{2}.$
Thus by Proposition 15.15, there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{0}$ of $G[V_{1}]$ with
$|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{e(G[V_{1}])-\frac{v(H)^{2}}{4}}{3}\geq\frac{(1-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(H)^{2}}{2}-\frac{v(H)^{2}}{4}}{3}\geq\left(\frac{1}{12}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)\cdot v(H)^{2}\geq 30\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n^{2},$
as desired where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Thus by Lemma 15.9 applied to $G_{0}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ with $\gamma:=15\sqrt{\alpha}$ , there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{1}$ of $G_{0}-\mathcal{E}$ that saturates $\mathcal{A}$ in $G_{0}$ such that letting $G_{1}:=(G_{0}-E(\mathcal{T}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ , we have that $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible and $\delta(G_{1})\geq(\frac{3}{4}-7\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(G_{1})$ .
Claim 12.7.7.
There exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $G_{1}-\mathcal{E}$ with $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 8\sqrt{\alpha}n$ such that letting $G_{2}:=G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ , we have that $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup and $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible.
Proof. Let $\mathcal{O}:=\{e\in E(G_{1}):e\in E(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(G_{1}[\mathcal{P}]))\}$ . Note that $\Delta(\mathcal{O})\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ by definition of $\mathcal{A}$ . Moreover $|V_{i}\cap V(G_{1})|\geq|V_{i}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-2\sqrt{\alpha})n\geq(1-4\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot v(G_{1})$ . Thus $G_{1}$ is $4\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced. Let $\gamma:=4\sqrt{\alpha}$ . For each $i\in[2]$ and $uv\in\mathcal{O}\cap E_{ii}(G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}])$ , we find by Claim .2 that
$|N_{G_{1}}(u)\cap N_{G_{1}}(v)\cap V_{3-i}^{*}|\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-6\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n\geq(1-8\sqrt{\alpha})\cdot\frac{v(G_{1})}{2}\geq\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot v(G_{1}),$
where the final inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. Hence by Lemma 15.17, there exists a triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ satisfying Lemma 15.17(i)-(iii). Condition (i) says that $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ consists only of cross triangles of $G_{1}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ and hence $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}^{*}]$ is cross-divisible since $G_{1}[P^{*}]$ is. Condition (ii) yields that $\mathcal{O}\subseteq E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ and hence $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{1}$ -blowup. Condition (iii) gives that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 4\sqrt{\gamma}\cdot n\leq 8\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . Hence $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{2}:=G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ . Next we utilize our parity-fixer. Let $\mathcal{Q}_{2}:=\{vu_{1}u_{4},vu_{2}u_{3}\}$ . Note given our construction, we have $(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2})\cap\mathcal{E}=\emptyset$ and hence $\mathcal{E}\subseteq E(G_{2})$ .
Claim 12.7.8.
Either $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ or $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -divisible.
Proof. First suppose $2\cdot e_{11}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])\equiv 0\pmod{4}$ . Then $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -divisible by Lemma 13.20 as desired.
So we assume that $2\cdot e_{11}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])\not\equiv 0\pmod{4}$ . Since $G_{2}$ is Eulerian, we have that $e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is even and hence so is $e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ . Since $2\cdot e_{11}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ is also even, we find that $2\cdot e_{11}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])\equiv 2\pmod{4}$ .
Thus we let $G_{2}^{\prime}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ . Note that $e_{11}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{11}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])$ . We also find that $e_{13}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{13}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])-1$ and $e_{14}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])=e_{14}(G_{2}[\mathcal{P}])+1$ . But then $2\cdot e_{11}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])+e_{13}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])-e_{14}(G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}])\equiv 2-1-(+1)\equiv 0\pmod{4}$ . Hence by Lemma 13.20, we find that $G_{2}^{\prime}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -divisible as desired.
$\Diamond$
If $G_{2}[\mathcal{P}]$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -divisible, let $G_{3}:=G_{2}$ and $\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{1}$ ; otherwise let $G_{3}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{Q}_{1})+E(\mathcal{Q}_{2})$ and $\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{2}$ . Finally we apply our idealized decomposition theorem, Theorem 14.7, to $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ as follows but first we check the conditions. Let $\varepsilon_{2}$ be as in Theorem 14.7 for $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ .
Note that $v(G_{3})\geq v(G)-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ . Given that $G[\mathcal{P}]$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced and that $\alpha$ is small enough, it follows that $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ is $\varepsilon_{2}$ -nearly-balanced. Similarly given the definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , it follows $G_{3}[\mathcal{P}]$ is an $\varepsilon_{2}$ -nearly-complete $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -blowup.
Hence by Theorem 14.7, $G_{3}$ has an $\mathcal{F}_{2}$ -decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ is a $K_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ as desired.
∎
15.7 Close to $T_{2}(n/2)+T_{2}(n/2)$
For this case, we first prove an in-between lemma which yields a decomposition when there are oddballs but they form an independent set and there are no interior edges as follows.
Theorem 15.19 (Idealized $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -decomposition with independent oddballs).
There exists $\varepsilon>0$ such that the following holds for all large enough $n$ : Let $\mathcal{F}_{3}:=\{T_{123},T_{124},T_{134},T_{234}\}$ . Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices and suppose that $G$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible and $\delta(G)\geq 300\cdot\varepsilon n$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}:=V(G)\setminus\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\Big\{v\in V_{i}:d_{j}(v)\geq(1-2\varepsilon)n/4\textrm{ for all }j\in[4]\setminus\{i\}\Big\}.$
If all of the following hold:
(a)
$|V_{i}|\geq(1-\varepsilon)n/4$ for all $i\in[4]$ ,
(b)
$|\mathcal{A}|\leq\varepsilon\cdot n$ and $\mathcal{A}$ is an independent set in $G$ , and
(c)
for each $i\in[4]$ , $v\in\mathcal{A}\cap V_{i}$ , and $j\in[4]\setminus\{i\}$ , we have that $d_{j}(v)\leq 0.49\cdot d(v)$ ,
then $G$ has an $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -decomposition.
Proof.
Let $\mathcal{P}:=(V_{1},\ldots,V_{4})$ be the partition of $G$ . Let $v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}$ be an enumeration of $\mathcal{A}$ . Let $S_{0}:=\emptyset$ . For each $i\in[k]$ , let $S_{i}:=\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{i}\}$ .
Claim 15.19.1.
For each $i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}$ , there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{i}$ of $S_{i}$ in $G$ .
Proof. We proceed by induction on $i$ . For $i=0$ , the empty decomposition suffices.
So we assume $i\geq 1$ . By induction, there exists a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{i-1}$ of $S_{i-1}$ in $G$ . Let $G_{i}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}_{i-1}))\setminus S_{i-1}$ . Let $n^{\prime}:=v(G_{i})$ . Note that $n^{\prime}=n-(i-1)\geq n-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-\varepsilon)n$ . We assume without loss of generality that $v_{i}\in V_{1}$ . Let $H:=G[N_{G}(v_{i})]$ and let $H^{\prime}:=G_{i}[N_{G_{i}}(v_{i})]=(H-E(\mathcal{T}_{i-1}))\setminus S_{i-1}$ . By assumption (b), we have that $\mathcal{A}$ is an independent set in $G$ . Thus $V(H^{\prime})\subseteq V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Also by assumption (c), we have that $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\leq 0.49\cdot d_{G}(v)$ and hence $|V(H)\cap V_{j}|\leq 0.49\cdot v(H)$ for each $j\in\{2,3,4\}$ . By definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , we find that for each $w\in V(H^{\prime})$ , we have
$d_{H^{\prime}}(w)\geq d_{H}(w)-2\cdot|S_{i-1}|\geq 0.51\cdot v(H)-2\varepsilon\cdot n\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot v(H^{\prime}),$
where the last inequality follows since $v(H^{\prime})\geq v(H)-\varepsilon\cdot n$ and $v(H)=d_{G}(v_{i})\geq\delta(G)\geq 300\varepsilon\cdot n$ . Thus by Pósa’s Theorem (in fact Dirac’s Theorem suffices here) we find that $H^{\prime}$ has a Hamilton cycle; as $v(H^{\prime})$ is even since $G$ is Eulerian (and hence so too is $G_{i}$ ), it follows that $H^{\prime}$ has a perfect matching $M_{i}$ . But then $M_{i}$ combined with the edges of $G_{i}$ incident with $v_{i}$ yields a triangle decomposition $\mathcal{T}_{i}^{\prime}$ of $v_{i}$ in $G_{i}$ . Hence $\mathcal{T}_{i}:=\mathcal{T}_{i-1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{i}^{\prime}$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G^{\prime}:=(G-E(\mathcal{T}_{k}))\setminus\mathcal{A}$ . Note that $G^{\prime}$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible. Let $\varepsilon_{3}$ be as in Theorem 14.7 for $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ . Note that $v(G^{\prime})\geq v(G)-|\mathcal{A}|\geq(1-\varepsilon)n$ . Given that $G$ is $\varepsilon$ -nearly-balanced and that $\varepsilon$ is small enough, it follows that $G^{\prime}$ is $\varepsilon_{3}$ -nearly-balanced. Similarly given the definition of $\mathcal{A}$ , it follows that $G^{\prime}$ is an $\varepsilon_{3}$ -nearly-complete $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -blowup.
Hence by Theorem 14.7, $G^{\prime}$ has an $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}_{k}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ is a $K_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ and in fact an $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ as desired (since the only triangles in $G$ are copies of $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ ).
∎
Next we prove our key proposition which shows that the numbers of matched triangles required for integral $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisibility is achievable, but first we define these matched triangles as follows.
Definition 15.20.
Let $M_{1}:=\{\{1,2\},\{3,4\}\}$ , $M_{2}:=\{\{1,3\},\{2,4\}\}$ , $M_{3}:=\{\{1,4\},\{2,3\}\}$ . Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph; for each $i\in[3]$ , we say a triangle $T$ is $i$ -matched if $T$ is a copy of $T_{jjk}$ for $\{j,k\}\in M_{i}$ . We consider the $M_{i}$ also as partitions and hence $G[M_{i}]$ denotes the $2$ -partitioned graph $G[V_{j_{1}}\cup V_{j_{2}},V_{k_{1}}\cup V_{k_{2}}]$ where $j_{1}j_{2}\neq k_{1}k_{2}\in M_{i}$ . For $i\in[3]$ , we say an edge $e$ is $i$ -matched if $e\in E_{jk}(G)$ and $jk\in M_{i}$ ; if $e\in E_{jj}(G)$ for some $j\in[4]$ , we say $e$ is $0$ -matched. For $i\in\{0\}\cup[3]$ , we let $M_{i}(G)$ denote the set of $i$ -matched edges and let $m_{i}(G):=|M_{i}(G)|$ .
Proposition 15.21.
Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned triangle-divisible graph on $n$ vertices with $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ .
For each $i\in[3]$ , let
$t_{i}:=\frac{\textrm{IntSurplus}(G[M_{i}])}{6}.$
Then $t_{i}$ is non-negative and integral and $\sum_{i\in[3]}t_{i}=m_{0}$ . Hence if there exists a triangle packing $\mathcal{T}$ of $G$ where for each $i\in[3]$ the number of $i$ -matched triangles in $\mathcal{T}$ equals $t_{i}$ , then $G-E(\mathcal{T})$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible.
Proof.
For each $i\in\{0\}\cup[3]$ , let $m_{i}:=m_{i}(G)$ . Note that for each $i\in[3]$ , we calculate that
$t_{i}=\frac{2m_{0}+2m_{i}-\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}m_{j}}{6}=\frac{2\cdot e(G)-3\cdot\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}m_{j}}{6}.$
For each $i\in[3]$ , $G[M_{i}]$ is a $2$ -partitioned triangle-divisible graph and hence by Proposition 15.2 we find that $t_{i}$ is integral; similarly since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , it follows that $t_{i}\geq 0$ . One then calculates that $\sum_{i\in[3]}t_{i}=3\cdot\frac{m_{0}}{3}+\sum_{i\in[3]}\frac{2m_{i}(G)-m_{i}(G)-m_{i}(G)}{6}=m_{0}$ as desired. Finally we prove that $G^{\prime}:=G-E(\mathcal{T})$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible. since $G$ is triangle-divisible and $\mathcal{T}$ is a triangle packing, it follows that $G^{\prime}$ is triangle-divisible and hence $G^{\prime}$ is Eulerian. Moreover, we find that $e_{ii}(G^{\prime})=0$ for all $i\in[4]$ and hence $G^{\prime}$ is an $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -blowup. Moreover, we find that $e_{12}(G^{\prime})+e_{34}(G^{\prime})=m_{1}-2\cdot t_{1}=\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}-2m_{0}}{3}$ . Hence by symmetry, we find that $e_{12}(G^{\prime})+e_{34}(G^{\prime})=e_{13}(G^{\prime})+e_{24}(G^{\prime})=e_{14}(G^{\prime})+e_{23}(G^{\prime})$ . Thus Lemma 13.22(i’) and (ii’) hold for $G^{\prime}$ . So by Lemma 13.22, we have that $G^{\prime}$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible as desired.
∎
Remark 15.22.
As per our discussion before about internal surplus, in order for $G[M_{i}]$ to admit a triangle-decomposition (equivalently for $G$ to admit one), such a decomposition requires the use of precisely $\frac{\textrm{IntSurplus}(G[M_{i}])}{6}=t_{i}$ interior triangles of $G[M_{i}]$ . Now an interior triangle of $G[M_{i}]$ is either an $i$ -matched triangle or a triangle inside $G[V_{j}]$ for some $j\in[4]$ . Since in general $G[V_{j}]$ may not contain any triangles (as in our current major case of being edit distance close to $T_{2}(n/2)+T_{2}(n/2)$ , we would have that $V_{j}$ is edit distance close to an independent set), it is natural then to seek a decomposition using $t_{i}$ $i$ -matched triangles.
To create our coloring of the internal edges with the desired properties, we use the following proposition. As a point of notation, for a $t$ -partitioned graph $G$ and $v\in V(G)$ and $i\in[t]$ , we let $\overline{d_{i}}(v):=|V_{i}|-d_{i}(v)$ . First we need the following definition.
Definition 15.23.
Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices with partition $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{4})$ . Let $i\in[3]$ , $v\in V_{j}$ for some $j\in[4]$ , and let $k$ be such that $jk\in M_{i}$ ; we define $m_{i}(v):=d_{k}(v)$ and $\overline{m_{i}}(v):=|V_{k}|-d_{k}(v)$ . We define
$t_{i}(v):=\left\lfloor 0.99\cdot\bigg(\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{m_{j}}(v)\bigg)\right\rfloor.$
Here then is our coloring proposition. It might be helpful to the reader if we first describe in words what each outcome guarantees and what its use will be as in the following remark.
Remark 15.24.
Recall that we need to color all interior edges while being careful how we color edges incident with abnormal vertices. For interior edges incident with two abnormal vertices, this needed particular care and was the subject of our earlier claims. After coloring those, we will color the edges incident with an abnormal vertex $v$ with $t_{i}(v)$ edges of color $i$ so as to achieve condition Theorem 15.19(c) and then color the remaining interior edges arbitrarily. This means the number of edges incident with $v$ of color $i$ will be at least $t_{i}(v)$ and at most $m_{0}(v)-\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}t_{j}(v)$ .
With this in mind here is a description of each outcome below. Outcome (i) says that $t_{i}(v)$ is non-negative which is clearly necessary to show. Outcome (ii) guarantees that our upper bound above on the number of edges colored $i$ is at most $m_{i}(v)$ , a necessary condition if we plan to use that number of $i$ -matched triangles at $v$ (where we have an additional buffer of $0.01n$ to account for small degrees losses). Outcome (iii) guarantees that new $i$ -degree after matching (which is at most $m_{i}(v)-t_{i}(v)$ ) is at most $0.485$ times the degree after matching (which is $d_{G}(v)-2\cdot m_{0}(v)$ ) so that Theorem 15.19(c) will hold (which was crucial to applying Pósa’s Theorem); note we included a buffer here as well ( $0.005n$ and $0.485$ as opposed to $0.49$ ). Outcome (iv) guarantees the sum of the $t_{i}(v)$ is at most the internal degree $m_{0}(v)$ , an obviously necessary condition, where again we have a buffer ( $0.995$ vs $1$ ). Finally outcome (v) guarantees that two of the $t_{i}(v)$ are large which in turn will show that the two of the global parameter $t_{i}$ ’s are linear in the number of abnormals which is key to finding a good color for interior abnormal-abnormal edges. This will follow since we will show the sum of $t_{i}(v)$ over the abnormal vertices is a lower bound for $t_{i}$ (indeed it would be almost equal if we dropped the $0.99$ in the definition of $t_{i}(v)$ and changed the $6$ in the denominator to $3$ ).
Proposition 15.25.
There exists $\gamma>0$ such that the following holds for all large enough $n$ . Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices with partition $(V_{1},\ldots,V_{4})$ and $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ such that $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced. Let $G$ be a $4$ -partitioned graph on $n$ vertices. Let $v\in V(G)$ . Let $i\in[3]$ . If $m_{0}(v)\geq\gamma^{2/3}\cdot n$ and $m_{0}(v)\leq m_{j}(v)+2\gamma\cdot n$ for all $j\in[3]$ , then all of the following hold:
(i)
$t_{i}(v)\geq 0$ ,
(ii)
$m_{i}(v)-0.01n\geq m_{0}(v)-\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}t_{j}(v)$ ,
(iii)
$m_{i}(v)-t_{i}(v)\leq 0.485(d_{G}(v)-2\cdot m_{0}(v))-0.005n$ ,
(iv)
$\sum_{j\in[3]}t_{j}(v)\leq 0.995\cdot m_{0}(v)$ ,
(v)
there exist distinct $j,k\in[3]$ such that $t_{j}(v),t_{k}(v)\geq\frac{1}{12}\cdot m_{0}(v)$ .
Proof.
We assume without loss of generality that $v\in V_{4}$ .
First we prove (i). Since $d_{G}(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ as $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ , we find that $d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n\geq 0$ . We also note that $\overline{m_{j}}(v)\geq 0$ for any $j\in[3]$ . Hence $t_{i}(v)\geq 0$ and (i) holds as desired.
Next we prove (ii). Now we calculate that
$\displaystyle m_{i}(v)+\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}t_{j}(v)$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.99\left(2\cdot\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+m_{i}(v)+\overline{m_{i}}(v)+\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\frac{\overline{m_{j}}(v)}{2}\right)$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.99\left(\frac{d_{G}(v)}{3}-\frac{n}{4}+|V_{i}|+\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\frac{\overline{m_{j}}(v)}{2}\right)$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.99\cdot\left(\frac{d_{G}(v)}{3}-2\gamma\cdot n.\right)$
First suppose that $m_{0}(v)\leq 0.2n$ . Since $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3n}{4}$ , the above is at least $0.99(0.25n-2\gamma n)\geq 0.22n\geq m_{0}(v)+0.01n$ as desired. So we assume $m_{0}(v)\geq 0.2n$ . But then by assumption, we have for each $j\in[3]$ that $m_{j}(v)\geq 0.2n-2\gamma\cdot n$ . Hence $d_{G}(v)\geq 0.8n-6\gamma\cdot n$ . But then the above is at least $0.99(0.266n-4\gamma\cdot n)>|V_{4}|+0.01n\geq m_{0}(v)+0.01n$ where we used that $\gamma$ is small enough and also that $|V_{4}|\leq\frac{n}{4}+2\gamma\cdot n$ as $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced. This proves (ii).
Next we prove (iii). Now we calculate that
$\displaystyle t_{i}(v)+0.485(d_{G}(v)-2\cdot m_{0}(v))$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.99\cdot\left(\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\overline{m_{j}}(v)\right)+0.485\left(\sum_{i\in[3]}m_{i}(v)-m_{0}(v)\right)$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.485\cdot\sum_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}(m_{j}(v)+\overline{m_{j}}(v))+0.99\cdot\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+0.485\cdot(m_{i}(v)-m_{0}(v))$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.485\cdot 2\cdot\left(\frac{n}{4}-\gamma\cdot n\right)+0.99\cdot\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+0.485\cdot(m_{i}(v)-m_{0}(v))$
$\displaystyle\geq(0.2425-\gamma)n+0.99\cdot\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+0.485\cdot(m_{i}(v)-m_{0}(v)).$
First suppose that $m_{i}(v)-m_{0}(v)\geq 0.026n$ . Then the above is at $(0.2425-\gamma)n+0.49(0.026n)\geq(0.25524-\gamma)n\geq m_{i}(v)+0.005n$ as desired where the last inequality follows since $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly balanced and $G$ is small enough. So we assume $m_{i}(v)\leq m_{0}(v)+0.026n$ . Next suppose that $m_{i}(v)<0.237n$ , but then the above is at least $(0.2425-\gamma)n+0.485(-2\gamma n)\geq(0.2425-2\gamma)n\geq m_{i}(v)+0.005n$ as desired since $\gamma$ is small enough. So we assume $m_{i}(v)\geq 0.237n$ and hence $m_{0}(v)\geq 0.211n$ from above. But then we also have that for $j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}$ , $m_{j}(v)\geq m_{0}(v)-2\gamma\cdot n$ . Thus $d_{G}(v)\geq 0.237n+0.211n+2\cdot(0.211n-2\gamma)n\geq(0.87-4\gamma)n$ . But now it follows that the last term in the large equation above is at least $(0.2425-\gamma)n+0.99\frac{(0.12-4\gamma)n}{6}+0.485(-2\gamma n)\geq(0.26-3\gamma)n\geq m_{i}(v)+0.005n$ as desired where for the last inequality we used that $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced and $\gamma$ is small enough. This proves (iii).
Next we prove (iv). Thus we calculate that
$\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[3]}t_{j}(v)$
$\displaystyle\leq 0.99\cdot\left(3\cdot\frac{d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n}{6}+\sum_{j\in[3]}(|V_{j}|-m_{j}(v))\right)$
$\displaystyle\leq 0.99\cdot\left(d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n+\sum_{j\in[3]}(|V_{j}|-m_{j}(v))\right)$
$\displaystyle\leq 0.99\cdot(m_{0}(v)+3\gamma\cdot n)\leq 0.995\cdot m_{0}(v),$
where the second to last inequality follows since $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced and the last inequality follows since $m_{0}(v)\geq\gamma^{2/3}\cdot n$ and $\gamma$ is small enough. This proves (iv).
Finally we prove (v). First suppose $d_{G}(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{2}{3}m_{0}(v)$ . Then it follows that for each $j\in[3]$ , we have that $t_{j}(v)\geq\lfloor 0.99\frac{2m_{0}(v)/3}{6}\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{12}m_{0}(v)$ as desired. So we assume $d_{G}(v)\leq\frac{3}{4}n+\frac{2}{3}m_{0}(v)$ . But then there exists $\ell\in[3]$ such that $m_{\ell}(v)\leq\frac{1}{3}(d_{G}(v)-m_{0}(v))\leq\frac{1}{3}(\frac{3}{4}n-\frac{m_{0}(v)}{3})=\frac{n}{4}-\frac{m_{0}(v)}{9}$ . Since $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced, it then follows that $\overline{m_{\ell}}(v)\geq\frac{m_{0}(v)}{9}-\gamma\cdot n\geq\frac{m_{0}(v)}{10}$ where the last inequality follows since $m_{0}(v)\geq\gamma^{2/3}\cdot n$ and $\gamma$ is small enough. But then each $j\in[3]\setminus\{\ell\}$ satisfies $t_{j}(v)\geq\lfloor 0.99\cdot\overline{m_{\ell}}(v)\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{12}m_{0}(v)$ as desired. This proves (v).
∎
We are now prepared to prove Theorem 12.8.
Proof of Theorem 12.8.
We choose $\alpha$ sufficiently small to satisfy various inequalities throughout the proof. Since $G$ is $\alpha$ -close to $\mathcal{F}_{3}(n)$ , there exists a partition $\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=(V_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,V_{4}^{\prime})$ of $V(G)$ with $\lfloor n/4\rfloor\leq|V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\lceil n/4\rceil$ for $i\in[4]$ such that $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(G[\mathcal{P^{\prime}}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ . Hence $\sum_{i<j\in[4]}e_{ij}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}])\geq(\frac{3}{8}-\alpha)n^{2}$ . Also note that the abnormality inequality also implies that $e(G)\leq(\frac{3}{8}+\alpha)n^{2}$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}^{\prime}:=\bigg\{v\in V(G):d_{\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}])}(v)\geq\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\bigg\}.$
Since $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ , it follows that $|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . For each $i\in[4]$ , let
$\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\prime}:=\{v\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{k<i}\mathcal{A}_{k}:|N(v)\cap V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq|N(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|\text{ for all }j\in[4]\setminus\{i\}\}.$
For each $i\in[4]$ , let
$V_{i}:=(V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\cup\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\prime}.$
Let $\mathcal{P}:=(V_{i}:i\in[4])$ . Note $\mathcal{P}$ is a partition of $V(G)$ . For the rest of the proof we consider $G$ to be the $4$ -partitioned graph $G[\mathcal{P}]$ .
Claim 12.8.1.
For each $i\in[4]$ , we have that $(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha})n\geq|V_{i}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ and hence $G$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -nearly-balanced.
Proof. For each $i\in[4]$ , we have that $|V_{i}|\geq|V_{i}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{n}{4}-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and similarly that $|V_{i}|\leq|V_{i}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{n}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and the claim follows.
$\Diamond$
Now for each $i\in[4]$ , we let
$\mathcal{A}_{i}:=\{v\in V_{i}:|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|\geq\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\},$
and
$\mathcal{A}:=\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\mathcal{A}_{i}.$
First we prove a series of claim concerning the degrees of vertices not in $\mathcal{A}$ and in $\mathcal{A}$ as well as upper bound the size of $\mathcal{A}$ .
Claim 12.8.2.
Let $i\in[4]$ . If $v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\cdot\alpha^{1/4})n$ .
Proof. Since $v\not\in\mathcal{A}$ , we find that $|N(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n$ . Note that $d_{G}(v)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ . By Claim .1, we have that $|N(v)\cap V_{k}|\leq(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha})n$ for each $k\in[4]\setminus\{i,j\}$ . Hence
$|N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq d_{G}(v)-|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|-\sum_{k\in[4]\setminus\{i,j\}}|N(v)\cap V_{k}|\geq\frac{3}{4}n-\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n-2\cdot\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}-2\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\right)n,$
where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.8.3.
$|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n$ .
Proof. Note that for each $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , we find that $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}^{\prime}|\geq|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|-|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\geq(\alpha^{1/4}-2\sqrt{\alpha})n\geq\frac{\alpha^{1/4}}{2}n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough. Hence $d_{\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(G[\mathcal{P}^{\prime}])}(v)\geq\frac{\alpha^{1/4}}{2}n$ . Since $e(\textrm{Abnormal}_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(G[\mathcal{P^{\prime}}]))\leq\alpha\cdot n^{2}$ , it follows that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq\frac{\alpha\cdot n^{2}}{\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n/2}=2\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.8.4.
Let $i\in[4]$ , $v\in V_{i}$ and $j\in N_{\mathcal{F}_{3}}(i)$ . If $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , then $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq\frac{d_{G}(v)}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|+4\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ .
Proof. By definition of $\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\prime}$ and since $\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{\prime}$ , it follows that $|N(v)\cap V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq|N(v)\cap V_{k}^{\prime}|$ for all $k\in[4]\setminus\{i\}$ . Hence $|N(v)\cap V_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{d_{G}(v)}{4}$ . But then $|N(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq\frac{d_{G}(v)}{4}+|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{d_{G}(v)}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ . Similarly $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|-|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\leq|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}^{\prime}|-|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}^{\prime}|+2|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\leq 2|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|\leq 4\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ as desired.
$\Diamond$
For cleaning, it will behoove us to categorize the types of edges to be cleaned as follows. Let
$\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{1}$
$\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{i<j\in[4]}\{e=uv\in E_{ij}(G):u,v\in\mathcal{A}\},$
$\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{2}$
$\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\{e=uv\in E_{ii}(G):u,v\in\mathcal{A}\},$
$\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{3}$
$\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\{e=uv\in E_{ii}(G):u,v\not\in\mathcal{A}\},$
$\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{4}$
$\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\{e=uv\in E_{ii}(G):u\in\mathcal{A}\text{ and }v\not\in\mathcal{A}\}.$
[That is $\mathcal{O}_{1}$ consists of ‘cross’ edges whose both ends are in $\mathcal{A}$ , $\mathcal{O}_{2}$ consists of ‘interior’ edges whose ends are both in $\mathcal{A}$ , $\mathcal{O}_{3}$ consists of interior edges whose ends are both not in $\mathcal{A}$ , and $\mathcal{O}_{4}$ consists of interior edges with one end in $\mathcal{A}$ and the other end not in $\mathcal{A}$ .]
We next prove a series of claims to establish properties we will need to create our coloring of the internal edges. First we prove that any edge in $\mathcal{O}_{2}$ has at least two color choices for which there are many options of matched triangle. To that end, we make the following definition. For each $e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ such that $u,v\in V_{i}$ for $i\in[3]$ , we define
$L(e):=\{j\in[4]\setminus\{i\}:|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{j}|,|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{k}|\geq 0.05n\}.$
Now we prove this has size at least $2$ as follows.
Claim 12.8.5.
If $e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ , then $|L(e)|\geq 2$ .
Proof. Suppose not. Let $e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ . We assume without loss of generality that $u,v\in V_{4}$ and that $1,2\not\in L(e)$ . Hence for each $i\in\{1,2\}$ , we find that $|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq 0.05n$ and hence $d_{i}(u)+d_{i}(v)\leq|V_{i}|+|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{i}|\leq(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha})n+0.05n$ . By Claim .4, we have that $d_{4}(u)\leq\frac{d_{G}(u)}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ and similarly $d_{4}(v)\leq\frac{d_{G}(v)}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n$ .
But then we find that
$d_{G}(u)+d_{G}(v)\leq 2\cdot\left(\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n+.05n\right)+2\cdot\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n+\frac{d_{G}(u)+d_{G}(v)}{4}+4\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n.$
Rearranging yields that
$d_{G}(u)+d_{G}(v)\leq\frac{4}{3}\cdot(1.1+12\sqrt{\alpha})n\leq(1.47+16\sqrt{\alpha})n<\frac{3}{2}n,$
where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. But this is a contradiction since $d_{G}(u)+d_{G}(v)\geq\frac{3}{2}n$ as $\delta(G)\geq\frac{3}{4}n$ .
$\Diamond$
For each $i\in[3]$ , let $t_{i}$ be as in Proposition 15.21 for $G$ . Next we prove that if we color edges around abnormal vertices with $t_{i}(v)$ edges of color $i$ and account for coloring $\mathcal{O}_{2}$ , we do not exceed $t_{i}$ (and hence have not ‘overcolored’ and so will be able to complete the coloring to having exactly $t_{i}$ edges of color $i$ ).
Claim 12.8.6.
For each $i\in[3]$ , we have that $\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}t_{i}(v)+|\mathcal{A}|^{2}\leq t_{i}$ .
Proof. We assume without loss of generality that $i=1$ . Now we calculate as follows that
$\displaystyle t_{1}$
$\displaystyle=\frac{\textrm{IntSurplus}(G[M_{1}])}{6}=\frac{2\cdot e(G)-3\cdot(m_{2}(G)+m_{3}(G))}{6}$
$\displaystyle=\frac{2\cdot e(G)-\frac{3}{4}\cdot n^{2}+\frac{3}{4}\cdot n^{2}-3(|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)+3\cdot\bigg((|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)-m_{2}(G)-m_{3}(G)\bigg)}{6}$
$\displaystyle={\sum_{v\in V(G)}\frac{(d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n)}{6}}+\frac{\frac{n^{2}}{4}-(|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)}{2}+\frac{(|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)-m_{2}(G)-m_{3}(G)}{2}$
$\displaystyle={\sum_{v\in V(G)}\frac{(d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n)}{6}}+\frac{\frac{n^{2}}{4}-(|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{v\in V(G)}\frac{\overline{m_{2}}(v)+\overline{m_{3}}(v)}{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{(d_{G}(v)-\frac{3}{4}n)}{6}+\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\overline{m_{2}}(v)+\overline{m_{3}}(v)}{2}-|\mathcal{A}|^{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq 1.005\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}t_{1}(v)-|\mathcal{A}|^{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}t_{1}(v)+0.005\cdot\frac{\alpha^{1/4}}{12}\cdot n\cdot|\mathcal{A}|-|\mathcal{A}|^{2}$
$\displaystyle\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}t_{1}(v)+|\mathcal{A}|^{2},$
where the first inequality follows since $\frac{n^{2}}{4}\geq(|V_{1}|+|V_{2}|)(|V_{3}|+|V_{4}|)$ and the fact that $\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{v\in V(G)}\frac{\overline{m_{2}}(v)+\overline{m_{3}}(v)}{2}\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\overline{m_{2}}(v)+\overline{m_{3}}(v)}{2}-|\mathcal{A}|^{2}$ , the second inequality follows from the definition of $t_{1}(v)$ , the third inequality follows from Proposition 15.25(v) since $\alpha$ is small enough, and the final inequality follows since $2|\mathcal{A}|^{2}\leq 0.005\cdot\frac{\alpha^{1/4}}{12}\cdot n\cdot|\mathcal{A}|$ as $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot 0.005\cdot\frac{\alpha^{1/4}}{12}\cdot n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough.
$\Diamond$
Next we prove a claim to show that at least two of the colors (read as two $t_{i}$ ’s) are large (and hence we will be able to color an edge $e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ with at least one color from $L(e)$ by pigeonhole).
Claim 12.8.7.
There exist distinct $i,j\in[3]$ such that $t_{i},t_{j}\geq\frac{1}{36}\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\cdot|\mathcal{A}|$ .
Proof. For each $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , let $B(v):=\{i\in[3]:t_{i}(v)\geq\frac{1}{36}\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\}$ . By Proposition 15.25(v), we have that $|B(v)|\geq 2$ for all $v\in\mathcal{A}$ . By pigeonhole, that there exists $S\subseteq[3]$ with $|S|=2$ such that for at least $\frac{|\mathcal{A}|}{3}$ vertices $v$ of $\mathcal{A}$ we have $S\subseteq B(v)$ . But then for each $i\in S$ , we find that $t_{i}\geq\frac{|\mathcal{A}|}{3}\cdot\frac{1}{12}\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n$ as desired. $\Diamond$
