Now we are prepared to prove our key coloring claim which colors the interior edges with the desired properties to achieve integral $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisibility so as to be able to apply Theorem 15.19.
Claim 12.8.8.
There exists a (not necessarily proper) $3$ -coloring $\varphi$ of $\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}$ such that all of the following hold:
(i)
for each $i\in[3]$ , we have that $|\{e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}:\varphi(e)=i\}|=t_{i}$ ,
(ii)
for each $uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ , we have $\varphi(e)\in L(e)$ ,
(iii)
for each $i\in[3]$ and $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , we have
$m_{i}(v)-0.01n\geq|\{e=vw\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}:\varphi(e)=i\}|\geq m_{i}(v)-0.485(d_{G}(v)-2\cdot m_{0}(v))+0.005n.$
Proof. Let $\mathcal{C}:=\{k\in[3]:t_{k}\geq\frac{1}{36}\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\cdot|\mathcal{A}|\}$ . By Claim .7, we find that $|\mathcal{C}|\geq 2$ . For each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ , we choose $\varphi(e)\in L(e)\cap\mathcal{C}$ ; note that such a color exists by the pigeonhole principle since $|\mathcal{C}|\geq 2$ and by Claim .5 we have that $|L(e)|\geq 2$ . Hence (ii) holds for $\varphi$ .
Next for each $v\in\mathcal{A}$ and $i\in[3]$ , we color $t_{i}(v)$ edges of $\mathcal{O}_{3}$ incident with $v$ with color $i$ (in a disjoint manner so that no edge receives two colors). Let $\gamma:=2\sqrt{\alpha}$ and hence $G$ is $\gamma$ -nearly-balanced by Claim .1. Note that as $v\in\mathcal{A}$ , we have by definition that $m_{0}(v)\geq\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\geq\gamma^{2/3}\cdot n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough. Thus by Proposition 15.25(i), $t_{i}(v)\geq 0$ and by definition $t_{i}(v)$ is integral. Similarly by Proposition 15.25(iv), $\sum_{j\in[3]}t_{j}(v)\leq 0.995\cdot m_{0}(v)$ . So this is indeed possible since $\alpha$ is small enough so that $|\mathcal{A}|\leq 0.005\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n\leq 0.005\cdot m_{0}(v)$ .
Now we color the remaining uncolored edges of $\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}$ arbitrarily such that the total number of edges of color $i$ is exactly $t_{i}$ . Such is possible by Claim .6. Then the upper bound in (iii) follows from Proposition 15.25(ii) and the lower bound in (iii) follows from Proposition 15.25(iii). Hence all of (i), (ii) and (iii) hold and $\varphi$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Claim 12.8.9.
There exists an $\mathcal{O}_{1}$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{1}$ of $G$ using only $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -copies such that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1})\leq 24\cdot\alpha^{3/8}\cdot n$ .
Proof. First note that for $i\in[3]$ and $v\in\mathcal{A}\cap V_{i}$ and $k\neq\ell\in[4]\setminus\{i\}$ , we have by Claim .4 letting $j=[4]\setminus\{i,k,\ell\}$ that
$|N_{G}(v)\cap(V_{k}\cup V_{\ell})|\geq d_{G}(v)-|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{i}|-|V_{j}|\geq\frac{3}{4}\cdot d_{G}(v)-2\sqrt{\alpha}\cdot n-\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n\geq\left(\frac{5}{16}-4\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n.$
Thus for each $u\in V_{i},v\in V_{j}$ such that $uv\in\mathcal{O}_{1}$ , we find that letting $k\neq\ell\in[4]\setminus\{i,j\}$ ,
$|N_{G}(u)\cap N_{G}(v)\cap(V_{k}\cup V_{\ell})|\geq 2\cdot\left(\frac{5}{16}-4\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n-2\cdot\left(\frac{1}{4}+2\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n\geq\left(\frac{1}{8}-12\sqrt{\alpha}\right)n.$
For each $i\in\{3\}$ , we let $\mathcal{O}_{1,i}:=\mathcal{O}_{1}\cap M_{i}(G)$ . Note that $\Delta(\mathcal{O}_{1,i})\leq\Delta(\mathcal{O}_{1})\leq|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\cdot\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n$ .
We claim that for each $i\in[3]$ , there exists an $\bigcup_{j\in[i]}\mathcal{O}_{1,j}$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{1,i}$ of $G$ using only $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -copies such that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1,i})\leq i\cdot 8\cdot\alpha^{3/8}\cdot n$ . We proceed by induction. Let $G_{1}:=G-\mathcal{O}_{1,2}-\mathcal{O}_{1,3}$ . For $i=1$ , such a $\mathcal{T}_{1,1}$ exists by Lemma 15.17 applied to $G_{1}[M_{1}]$ with $\gamma:=2\cdot\alpha^{3/4}$ since $\alpha$ is small enough. For $i\in\{2,3\}$ , by Lemma 15.17 applied to $G_{i}:=G[M_{i}]-E(\mathcal{T}_{i-1})-\bigcup_{j\in[3]\setminus\{i\}}\mathcal{O}_{1,j}$ and $\gamma$ since $\alpha$ is small enough, there exists an $\mathcal{O}_{1,i}$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{1,i}^{\prime}$ of $G_{i}$ using only $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -copies in $G$ such that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1,i}^{\prime})\leq 8\cdot\alpha^{3/8}\cdot n$ and hence $\mathcal{T}_{1,i}:=\mathcal{T}_{1,i-1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{1,i}^{\prime}$ is as claimed.
But then $\mathcal{T}_{1}:=\mathcal{T}_{1,3}$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{1}:=G-E(\mathcal{T}_{1})$ .
Claim 12.8.10.
There exists an $(\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3})$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ of $G_{1}$ such that $\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 4\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n$ and for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}$ , the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched.
Proof. For each $i\in[3]$ , let $\mathcal{O}_{2,i}:=\{e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}:\varphi(e)=i\}$ . For each $i\in[3]$ , let $G_{1,i}$ be the $2$ -partitioned graph $V(G_{1,i}):=V(G)$ , $E(G_{1,i}):=M_{i}(G_{1})$ and partition $(V_{j_{1}}\cup V_{k_{1}},V_{j_{1}}\cup V_{k_{2}})$ where $M_{i}=\{j_{1}j_{2},k_{1}k_{2}\}$ .
We claim that for each $i\in[3]$ , $e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}$ with $u,v\in V_{i}$ and letting $j$ be such that $ij\in M_{\varphi(e)}$ , we have that $|N_{G_{1}}(u)\cap N_{G_{1}}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq 0.04n$ . To see this, first suppose $u,v\in\mathcal{O}_{3}$ , that is , $u,v\not\in\mathcal{A}$ . Then by Claim .4 we find that $|N_{G}(u)\cap V_{j}|,|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\cdot\alpha^{1/4})n$ and hence $|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-6\cdot\alpha^{1/4})n$ . Since $|N_{G}(w)\setminus N_{G_{1}}(w)|\leq\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1})\leq 24\cdot\alpha^{3/8}\cdot n$ for all $w\in V(G)$ , it follows that $|N_{G_{1}}(u)\cap N_{G_{1}}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq 0.04n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough.
So we assume $uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}$ , that is $u,v\in\mathcal{A}$ . Then by Claim .8(ii) we find that $|N(u)\cap N(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq.05n$ . Since $|N_{G}(w)\setminus N_{G_{1}}(w)|\leq\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1})\leq 24\cdot\alpha^{3/8}\cdot n$ for all $w\in V(G)$ , it follows that $|N_{G_{1}}(u)\cap N_{G_{1}}(v)\cap V_{j}|\geq 0.04n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough.
Thus by Lemma 15.17 applied to $G_{1,i}$ with $\gamma:=\alpha^{1/4}$ since $\alpha$ is small enough, there exists an $\mathcal{O}_{2,i}$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{2,i}$ of $G_{1,i}$ consisting only of cross triangles of $G_{i}$ and such that $\Delta(T_{2,i})\leq 4\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n$ . By construction of $G_{i}$ , it follows that every $\mathcal{T}_{2,i}$ is $i$ -matched. Note also by construction that the $T_{2,i}$ are pairwise disjoint. Hence $\mathcal{T}_{2}:=\bigcup_{i\in[3]}T_{2,i}$ is as desired.
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{2}:=G_{1}-E(\mathcal{T}_{2})$ .
Claim 12.8.11.
There exists an $\mathcal{O}_{4}$ -encompassed triangle-packing $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ of $G_{2}$ such that for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{4}$ , the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched.
Proof. Let $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ be a triangle-packing of $G_{2}$ such that every triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ contains exactly one edge in $\mathcal{O}_{4}$ and for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{3}$ the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched, and subject to that $|\mathcal{T}_{3}|$ is maximized. If $|\mathcal{T}_{3}|=|\mathcal{O}_{4}|$ , then $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ is as desired.
So we assume there exists $e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{4}\setminus E(\mathcal{T}_{3})$ . We assume without loss of generality that $u\in\mathcal{A}\cap V_{1}$ and $v\in V_{1}\setminus\mathcal{A}$ and that $\varphi(e)=1$ . By Claim .4, we find that $|N_{G}(v)\cap V_{2}|\geq(\frac{1}{4}-2\cdot\alpha^{1/4})n$ and hence $|V_{2}\setminus N_{G}(v)|\leq 3\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n$ .
Note that for each $w\in V(G)$ , we have that $|N_{G}(w)\setminus N_{G_{2}}(w)|\leq\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2})\leq 28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n$ . Let $G_{3}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{T}_{3})$ . On the other hand by construction, we have that
$\displaystyle|N_{G_{3}}(u)\cap V_{2}|$
$\displaystyle\geq|N_{G_{2}}(u)\cap V_{2}|-|\{e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}:\varphi(e)=1\}|$
$\displaystyle\geq|N_{G}(u)\cap V_{2}|-28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n-|\{e=uv\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4}:\varphi(e)=1\}|$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.01n-28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n,$
where the last inequality follows from the upper bound in Claim .8(iii). Since $|N_{G_{2}}(v)\setminus N_{G_{3}}(v)|\leq 2\cdot|\mathcal{A}|\leq 4\cdot\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n$ as $v\not\in\mathcal{A}$ , we find that
$\displaystyle|N_{G_{3}}(u)\cap N_{G_{3}}(v)\cap V_{2}|$
$\displaystyle\geq|N_{G_{3}}(u)\cap V_{2}|-|N_{G_{2}}(v)\setminus N_{G_{3}}(v)|-|N_{G}(v)\setminus N_{G_{2}}(v)|-|V_{2}\setminus N_{G}(v)|$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.01n-28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n-4\cdot\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n-28\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n-3\cdot\alpha^{1/4}\cdot n$
$\displaystyle\geq 0.01n-63\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n>0,$
where the last inequality follows since $\alpha$ is small enough. Hence there exists $z\in N_{G_{3}}(u)\cap N_{G_{3}}(v)\cap V_{2}$ . Let $\mathcal{T}_{3}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{T}_{3}\cup\{uvz\}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}_{3}^{\prime}$ is a triangle-packing of $G_{2}$ such that every triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}^{\prime}$ contains exactly one edge in $\mathcal{O}_{4}$ and for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{4}$ the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched (since $T$ is $1$ -matched and $\varphi(uv)=1$ ). Since $|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}|>|\mathcal{T}|$ , this contradicts the choice of $\mathcal{T}$ .
$\Diamond$
Let $G_{3}:=G_{2}-E(\mathcal{T}_{3})$ . Let $\mathcal{T}:=\bigcup_{i\in[3]}\mathcal{T}_{i}$ and hence $G_{3}=G-E(\mathcal{T})$ . By Claim .10, $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ is an $(\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3})$ -encompassed triangle packing of $G$ such that for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}$ , the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{2}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched. Similarly by Claim .11, $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ is an $\mathcal{O}_{4}$ -encompassed triangle packing of $G$ such that for each $e\in\mathcal{O}_{4}$ , the triangle in $\mathcal{T}_{3}$ containing $e$ is $\varphi(e)$ -matched. Since $\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3}=\emptyset$ by construction, it follows from Claim .8(i) that for each $i\in[3]$ , the number of $i$ -matched triangles in $\mathcal{T}$ equals $t_{i}$ . Hence by Proposition 15.21, we find that $G_{3}$ is integrally $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -divisible.
Let $\varepsilon$ be as in Theorem 15.19. Since $G$ is $2\sqrt{\alpha}$ -balanced by Claim .1, we find that Theorem 15.19(a) holds for $G_{3}$ . Let
$\mathcal{A}_{3}:=V(G)\setminus\bigcup_{i\in[4]}\Big\{v\in V_{i}:d_{G_{3},j}(v)\geq(1-2\varepsilon)n/4\textrm{ for all }j\in[4]\setminus\{i\}\Big\}.$
Note that for each $w\in V(G)\setminus\mathcal{A}$ , we have that $|N_{G}(w)\setminus N_{G_{3}}(w)|\leq\Delta(\mathcal{T})\leq\Delta(\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2})+|\mathcal{A}|\leq 28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n+2\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n$ . Since $\alpha$ is small enough, it then follows that $\mathcal{A}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{A}$ . Thus $|\mathcal{A}_{3}|\leq|\mathcal{A}|\leq 2\alpha^{3/4}\cdot n\leq\varepsilon n$ since $\alpha$ is small enough. Furthermore, $\mathcal{A}$ is an independent set in $G_{3}$ as $(\mathcal{O}_{1}\cup\mathcal{O}_{2}\cup\mathcal{O}_{3}\cup\mathcal{O}_{4})\cap E(G_{3})=\emptyset$ ; hence it follows that $\mathcal{A}_{4}$ is an independent set in $G$ . Thus Theorem 15.19(b) holds for $G_{3}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{4}$ .
Finally Theorem 15.19(c) holds for $G_{3}$ and $\mathcal{A}_{3}$ (in fact for $\mathcal{A}$ ) by the lower bound in Claim .8(c) and the fact that $\Delta(T_{1}\cup T_{2})\leq 28\cdot\alpha^{1/8}\cdot n$ and $\alpha$ is small enough. Thus by Theorem 15.19, there exists an $\mathcal{F}_{3}$ -decomposition $\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ of $G_{3}$ . But then $\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{0}$ is a $K_{3}$ -decomposition of $G$ as desired.
∎

Instructions for reporting errors
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
methods listed below:
Click the "Report Issue" (
) button, located in the page header.
Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
all.
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion, and welcome developer contributions.
